H_{n}=\frac{2}{n}\sum_{1\leq i<k\leq n}\bm{\xi}_{i}^{\top}\bm{\xi}_{k}=\sum_{k=2}^{n}M_{n,k},\qquad M_{n,k}:=\frac{2}{n}\bm{R}_{k-1}^{\top}\bm{\xi}_{k}.

Since
\mathbb{E}(\bm{\xi}_{k})=0
and
\bm{\xi}_{k}
is independent of
\mathcal{F}_{k-1}
,
\{M_{n,k},\mathcal{F}_{k}\}
is a martingale difference array. Moreover,

\mathbb{E}(\bm{\xi}_{i}\bm{\xi}_{i}^{\top})=\mathbf{I}_{p},\qquad\mathbb{E}\|\bm{\xi}_{i}\|^{2}=p,\qquad\mathbb{E}\|\bm{\xi}_{i}\|^{4}=O(p^{2}),\qquad\mathbb{E}[(\bm{\xi}_{i}^{\top}\bm{\xi}_{k})^{2}]=p\quad(i\neq k).

The predictable quadratic variation is

V_{n}:=\sum_{k=2}^{n}\mathbb{E}(M_{n,k}^{2}\mid\mathcal{F}_{k-1})=\frac{4}{n^{2}}\sum_{k=2}^{n}\|\bm{R}_{k-1}\|^{2}.

Using

\|\bm{R}_{k-1}\|^{2}=\sum_{i=1}^{k-1}\|\bm{\xi}_{i}\|^{2}+2\sum_{1\leq i<j\leq k-1}\bm{\xi}_{i}^{\top}\bm{\xi}_{j}

and
\mathbb{E}(\bm{\xi}_{i}^{\top}\bm{\xi}_{j})=0
for
i\neq j
, we get

\mathbb{E}\|\bm{R}_{k-1}\|^{2}=(k-1)p,\qquad\mathbb{E}(V_{n})=\frac{4}{n^{2}}\sum_{k=2}^{n}(k-1)p=\frac{2(n-1)}{n}p.

Write

V_{n}=A_{n}^{\star}+B_{n}^{\star},\qquad A_{n}^{\star}=\frac{4}{n^{2}}\sum_{i=1}^{n-1}(n-i)\|\bm{\xi}_{i}\|^{2},\qquad B_{n}^{\star}=\frac{8}{n^{2}}\sum_{1\leq i<j\leq n-1}(n-j)\bm{\xi}_{i}^{\top}\bm{\xi}_{j}.

Since
\|\bm{\xi}_{i}\|^{2}
are independent,

\mathrm{Var}(A_{n}^{\star})\leq\frac{16}{n^{4}}\sum_{i=1}^{n-1}(n-i)^{2}\mathbb{E}\|\bm{\xi}_{i}\|^{4}=O\!\left(\frac{p^{2}}{n}\right).

Likewise, if
Y_{ij}=\bm{\xi}_{i}^{\top}\bm{\xi}_{j}
, then
\mathbb{E}(Y_{ij})=0
and
\mathbb{E}(Y_{ij}Y_{rs})=0
unless
(i,j)=(r,s)
, so

\mathrm{Var}(B_{n}^{\star})=\frac{64}{n^{4}}\sum_{1\leq i<j\leq n-1}(n-j)^{2}\mathbb{E}(Y_{ij}^{2})=\frac{64p}{n^{4}}\sum_{j=2}^{n-1}(j-1)(n-j)^{2}=O(p).

Therefore

\mathrm{Var}(V_{n})=O\!\left(\frac{p^{2}}{n}\right)+O(p)=o(p^{2}),\qquad\frac{V_{n}}{2p}\xrightarrow{p}1.

To verify the Lindeberg condition, note that conditionally on
\mathcal{F}_{k-1}
,
\bm{R}_{k-1}
is deterministic, and for any fixed
\bm{a}\in\mathbb{R}^{p}
,

\mathbb{E}[(\bm{a}^{\top}\bm{\xi}_{k})^{4}]=p^{2}\mathbb{E}(Z_{k}^{4})\mathbb{E}[(\bm{a}^{\top}\bm{U}_{k})^{4}]\leq Cp^{2}\cdot\frac{\|\bm{a}\|^{4}}{p^{2}}=C\|\bm{a}\|^{4},

where we used the spherical fourth-moment identity
\mathbb{E}[(\bm{a}^{\top}\bm{U}_{k})^{4}]=3\|\bm{a}\|^{4}/[p(p+2)]
. Hence

\mathbb{E}(M_{n,k}^{4})\leq\frac{C}{n^{4}}\mathbb{E}\|\bm{R}_{k-1}\|^{4}.

Now

\|\bm{R}_{k-1}\|^{4}\leq 2\left(\sum_{i=1}^{k-1}\|\bm{\xi}_{i}\|^{2}\right)^{2}+8\left(\sum_{1\leq i<j\leq k-1}\bm{\xi}_{i}^{\top}\bm{\xi}_{j}\right)^{2},

so independence, centering,
\mathbb{E}\|\bm{\xi}_{i}\|^{4}=O(p^{2})
, and
\mathbb{E}[(\bm{\xi}_{i}^{\top}\bm{\xi}_{j})^{2}]=p
imply

\mathbb{E}\|\bm{R}_{k-1}\|^{4}=O\bigl((k-1)^{2}p^{2}\bigr).

Consequently,

\mathbb{E}(M_{n,k}^{4})\leq\frac{C}{n^{4}}(k-1)^{2}p^{2},\qquad\sum_{k=2}^{n}\mathbb{E}(M_{n,k}^{4})=O\!\left(\frac{p^{2}}{n}\right).

Therefore

\frac{1}{(2p)^{2}}\sum_{k=2}^{n}\mathbb{E}(M_{n,k}^{4})\to 0,

and for every
\varepsilon>0
,

\frac{1}{2p}\sum_{k=2}^{n}\mathbb{E}\left[M_{n,k}^{2}\mathbf{1}\{|M_{n,k}|>\varepsilon\sqrt{2p}\}\right]\leq\frac{1}{\varepsilon^{2}(2p)^{2}}\sum_{k=2}^{n}\mathbb{E}(M_{n,k}^{4})\to 0.

Thus the Lindeberg condition holds. Hall and Heyde’s martingale CLT
(
14
, Corollary 3.1)
now yields

\frac{H_{n}}{\sqrt{2p}}=\frac{\sum_{k=2}^{n}M_{n,k}}{\sqrt{2p}}\xrightarrow{d}N(0,1).

∎

Proposition A.1
.

Under Assumption
3.1
. Suppose furthermore that

p\to\infty,\qquad p=O(n^{\kappa})\quad\text{for some }\kappa\in(0,2).

Define the idealized sum statistic

\widetilde{T}_{\mathrm{sum}}=\frac{np}{\hat{\sigma}_{L}^{2}}\sum_{j=1}^{p}\hat{C}_{j}^{2}.

Then

\frac{\widetilde{T}_{\mathrm{sum}}-p}{\sqrt{2p}}\xrightarrow{d}N(0,1).

Proof.

By Lemma
A.4
,

\widetilde{T}_{\mathrm{sum}}=\widetilde{T}_{\mathrm{sum}}^{\circ}+o_{p}(\sqrt{p}).

By Lemma
A.5
,

\widetilde{T}_{\mathrm{sum}}^{\circ}-p=\frac{p\bar{Z}^{2}+H_{n}}{1+A_{n}-\bar{Z}^{2}},

with
(p\bar{Z}^{2})/\sqrt{2p}=o_{p}(1)
and
1+A_{n}-\bar{Z}^{2}\to 1
in probability. Lemma
A.6
then gives

\frac{\widetilde{T}_{\mathrm{sum}}^{\circ}-p}{\sqrt{2p}}\xrightarrow{d}N(0,1).

Combining these two facts and applying Slutsky’s theorem proves the proposition.
∎

Lemma A.7
.

Let
\eta_{ij}=U_{ij}^{2}-p^{-1}
. Then, for any deterministic coefficients
a_{1},\dots,a_{p}
,

\mathbb{E}\left(\sum_{j=1}^{p}a_{j}\eta_{ij}\right)^{2}=\frac{2}{p(p+2)}\sum_{j=1}^{p}a_{j}^{2}-\frac{2}{p^{2}(p+2)}\left(\sum_{j=1}^{p}a_{j}\right)^{2}\leq\frac{2}{p(p+2)}\sum_{j=1}^{p}a_{j}^{2}.

Proof.

For
\bm{U}_{i}\sim\mathrm{Unif}(\mathbb{S}^{p-1})
, the standard sphere moments are

\mathbb{E}(U_{ij}^{2})=\frac{1}{p},\qquad\mathbb{E}(U_{ij}^{4})=\frac{3}{p(p+2)},\qquad\mathbb{E}(U_{ij}^{2}U_{im}^{2})=\frac{1}{p(p+2)}\quad(j\neq m).

Hence

\mathbb{E}(\eta_{ij}^{2})=\frac{2(p-1)}{p^{2}(p+2)},\qquad\mathbb{E}(\eta_{ij}\eta_{im})=-\frac{2}{p^{2}(p+2)}\quad(j\neq m).

Expanding the square and substituting these identities gives the stated formula.
∎

Lemma A.8
.

Define

H_{ik}:=\sum_{j=1}^{p}U_{kj}^{2}\eta_{ij},\qquad G_{ik\ell}:=\sum_{j=1}^{p}U_{kj}U_{\ell j}\eta_{ij},

for
i\neq k
and pairwise distinct
i,k,\ell
. Then

\mathbb{E}(H_{ik}^{2})=O(p^{-3}),\qquad\mathbb{E}(G_{ik\ell}^{2})=O(p^{-3}).

Moreover,
H_{ik}
is degenerate in both arguments
i
and
k
, and
G_{ik\ell}
is degenerate in each of its three arguments.

Proof.

Since
\mathbb{E}(\eta_{ij})=0
, conditioning on
\bm{U}_{k}
gives

\mathbb{E}(H_{ik}\mid\bm{U}_{k})=\sum_{j=1}^{p}U_{kj}^{2}\mathbb{E}(\eta_{ij})=0.

Conditioning on
\bm{U}_{i}
gives

\mathbb{E}(H_{ik}\mid\bm{U}_{i})=\frac{1}{p}\sum_{j=1}^{p}\eta_{ij}=\frac{1}{p}(\|\bm{U}_{i}\|^{2}-1)=0.

Thus
H_{ik}
is completely degenerate. The same argument, together with
\mathbb{E}(U_{kj})=0
, shows that
G_{ik\ell}
is degenerate in each argument.

For the second moments, conditioning on
\bm{U}_{k}
and applying Lemma
A.7
with
a_{j}=U_{kj}^{2}
yields

\mathbb{E}(H_{ik}^{2}\mid\bm{U}_{k})\leq\frac{2}{p(p+2)}\sum_{j=1}^{p}U_{kj}^{4}.

Taking expectation and using
\mathbb{E}\sum_{j=1}^{p}U_{kj}^{4}=3/(p+2)
gives
\mathbb{E}(H_{ik}^{2})=O(p^{-3})
. Likewise, conditioning on
(\bm{U}_{k},\bm{U}_{\ell})
and applying Lemma
A.7
with
a_{j}=U_{kj}U_{\ell j}
gives

\mathbb{E}(G_{ik\ell}^{2}\mid\bm{U}_{k},\bm{U}_{\ell})\leq\frac{2}{p(p+2)}\sum_{j=1}^{p}U_{kj}^{2}U_{\ell j}^{2}.

Taking expectation and using independence of
\bm{U}_{k}
and
\bm{U}_{\ell}
yields
\mathbb{E}(G_{ik\ell}^{2})=O(p^{-3})
.
∎

Lemma A.9
.

Under Assumption
3.1
. Recall that

\eta_{ij}=U_{ij}^{2}-\frac{1}{p},\qquad W_{j}^{(-i)}=\frac{1}{n}\sum_{k\neq i}Z_{k}U_{kj},

and define

A_{1n}=-p^{2}\sigma_{L}^{2}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\sum_{j=1}^{p}\bigl(W_{j}^{(-i)}\bigr)^{2}\eta_{ij}.

Then

\mathbb{E}\left\{\left(\frac{A_{1n}}{\sigma_{L}^{2}}\right)^{2}\right\}=O\!\left(\frac{p}{n}\right).

In particular,

\frac{A_{1n}}{\sigma_{L}^{2}\sqrt{p}}\xrightarrow{p}0.

Proof.

For each fixed
i
and
j
,

\bigl(W_{j}^{(-i)}\bigr)^{2}=\frac{1}{n^{2}}\left(\sum_{k\neq i}Z_{k}^{2}U_{kj}^{2}+2\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}k<\ell\\
k,\ell\neq i\end{subarray}}Z_{k}Z_{\ell}U_{kj}U_{\ell j}\right).

Hence
A_{1n}=B_{2n}+B_{3n}
, where

B_{2n}:=-\frac{p^{2}\sigma_{L}^{2}}{n^{2}}\sum_{i\neq k}Z_{k}^{2}H_{ik},\qquad B_{3n}:=-\frac{2p^{2}\sigma_{L}^{2}}{n^{2}}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}k<\ell\\
k,\ell\neq i\end{subarray}}Z_{k}Z_{\ell}G_{ik\ell}.

Since

H_{ik}=\sum_{j=1}^{p}U_{kj}^{2}\eta_{ij}=\sum_{j=1}^{p}U_{ij}^{2}U_{kj}^{2}-\frac{1}{p},

we have
H_{ik}=H_{ki}
. Put
X_{ik}=Z_{k}^{2}H_{ik}
. The degeneracy of
H_{ik}
implies that
\mathbb{E}(X_{ik}X_{i^{\prime}k^{\prime}})=0
unless either
(i,k)=(i^{\prime},k^{\prime})
or
(i,k)=(k^{\prime},i^{\prime})
. Therefore

\mathbb{E}\left\{\left(\frac{B_{2n}}{\sigma_{L}^{2}}\right)^{2}\right\}=\frac{p^{4}}{n^{4}}\left\{\sum_{i\neq k}\mathbb{E}(X_{ik}^{2})+\sum_{i\neq k}\mathbb{E}(X_{ik}X_{ki})\right\}.

By independence of the
Z
’s and
U
’s, finiteness of
\mathbb{E}(Z_{k}^{4})
, and Lemma
A.8
, both
\mathbb{E}(X_{ik}^{2})
and
\mathbb{E}(X_{ik}X_{ki})
are
O(p^{-3})
. Since there are
O(n^{2})
ordered pairs
(i,k)
with
i\neq k
,

\mathbb{E}\left\{\left(\frac{B_{2n}}{\sigma_{L}^{2}}\right)^{2}\right\}=O\!\left(\frac{p^{4}}{n^{4}}\cdot n^{2}\cdot p^{-3}\right)=O\!\left(\frac{p}{n^{2}}\right).

Next, let

\mathcal{I}_{3}:=\{(i,k,\ell):1\leq i\leq n,\ 1\leq k<\ell\leq n,\ k,\ell\neq i\},\qquad Y_{ik\ell}:=Z_{k}Z_{\ell}G_{ik\ell}.

Then

B_{3n}=-\frac{2p^{2}\sigma_{L}^{2}}{n^{2}}\sum_{(i,k,\ell)\in\mathcal{I}_{3}}Y_{ik\ell}.

If
\{i,k,\ell\}\neq\{i^{\prime},k^{\prime},\ell^{\prime}\}
, then among the six indices there is at least one appearing exactly once. Conditioning on all other variables and using the degeneracy of
G_{ik\ell}
together with
\mathbb{E}(Z_{r})=0
shows that

\mathbb{E}(Y_{ik\ell}Y_{i^{\prime}k^{\prime}\ell^{\prime}})=0.

Thus only pairs with the same underlying triple can contribute. The number of such ordered pairs is
O(n^{3})
, and each contribution is
O(p^{-3})
by Cauchy–Schwarz and Lemma
A.8
. Therefore

\mathbb{E}\left\{\left(\frac{B_{3n}}{\sigma_{L}^{2}}\right)^{2}\right\}=O\!\left(\frac{p^{4}}{n^{4}}\cdot n^{3}\cdot p^{-3}\right)=O\!\left(\frac{p}{n}\right).

Finally,

\left|\mathbb{E}\left(\frac{B_{2n}B_{3n}}{\sigma_{L}^{4}}\right)\right|\leq\left[\mathbb{E}\left\{\left(\frac{B_{2n}}{\sigma_{L}^{2}}\right)^{2}\right\}\right]^{1/2}\left[\mathbb{E}\left\{\left(\frac{B_{3n}}{\sigma_{L}^{2}}\right)^{2}\right\}\right]^{1/2}=O\!\left(\frac{p}{n^{3/2}}\right).

Since
A_{1n}=B_{2n}+B_{3n}
,

\mathbb{E}\left\{\left(\frac{A_{1n}}{\sigma_{L}^{2}}\right)^{2}\right\}=\mathbb{E}\left\{\left(\frac{B_{2n}}{\sigma_{L}^{2}}\right)^{2}\right\}+\mathbb{E}\left\{\left(\frac{B_{3n}}{\sigma_{L}^{2}}\right)^{2}\right\}+2\mathbb{E}\left(\frac{B_{2n}B_{3n}}{\sigma_{L}^{4}}\right)=O\!\left(\frac{p}{n}\right).

Markov’s inequality then yields
A_{1n}/(\sigma_{L}^{2}\sqrt{p})\to 0
in probability.
∎

Lemma A.10
.

Under Assumption
3.1
. Suppose furthermore that

p\to\infty,\qquad p=O(n^{\kappa})\quad\text{for some }\kappa\in(0,2).

Define

R_{U,n}:=n\sum_{j=1}^{p}\hat{C}_{j}^{2}\bigl(\hat{\sigma}_{U,j}^{-2}-p\bigr).

Then

\frac{R_{U,n}}{\sigma_{L}^{2}}=o_{p}(\sqrt{p}).

Proof.

Write

\Delta_{j}:=\hat{\sigma}_{U,j}^{2}-\frac{1}{p}.

By Lemma
A.2
,

\max_{1\leq j\leq p}|\Delta_{j}|=O_{p}\!\left(\frac{\sqrt{\log p}}{p\sqrt{n}}+\frac{\log p}{np}\right),\qquad\max_{1\leq j\leq p}|p\Delta_{j}|=O_{p}\!\left(\sqrt{\frac{\log p}{n}}+\frac{\log p}{n}\right)=o_{p}(1).

Hence, on an event whose probability tends to one,

\hat{\sigma}_{U,j}^{-2}=p-p^{2}\Delta_{j}+\rho_{j},\qquad|\rho_{j}|\leq Cp^{3}\Delta_{j}^{2},

so that

\max_{1\leq j\leq p}|\rho_{j}|=O_{p}\!\left(\frac{p\log p}{n}+\frac{p(\log p)^{2}}{n^{2}}\right).

Decompose

R_{U,n}=R_{1n}+R_{2n},\qquad R_{1n}:=-p^{2}n\sum_{j=1}^{p}\hat{C}_{j}^{2}\Delta_{j},\qquad R_{2n}:=n\sum_{j=1}^{p}\hat{C}_{j}^{2}\rho_{j}.

Now

\hat{C}_{j}=\sigma_{L}(W_{j}-\bar{Z}\bar{U}_{j}),\qquad\|\hat{\bm{C}}\|^{2}\leq 2\sigma_{L}^{2}\|\bm{W}_{n}\|^{2}+2\sigma_{L}^{2}\bar{Z}^{2}\|\bar{\bm{U}}\|^{2}.

Since
\|\bm{W}_{n}\|^{2}=O_{p}(n^{-1})
,
\bar{Z}=O_{p}(n^{-1/2})
, and
\|\bar{\bm{U}}\|^{2}=O_{p}(n^{-1})
, we have
\|\hat{\bm{C}}\|^{2}=\sigma_{L}^{2}O_{p}(n^{-1})
, hence

\frac{n}{\sigma_{L}^{2}}\sum_{j=1}^{p}\hat{C}_{j}^{2}=O_{p}(1).

Therefore

\frac{|R_{2n}|}{\sigma_{L}^{2}}\leq\left(\frac{n}{\sigma_{L}^{2}}\sum_{j=1}^{p}\hat{C}_{j}^{2}\right)\max_{1\leq j\leq p}|\rho_{j}|=O_{p}\!\left(\frac{p\log p}{n}+\frac{p(\log p)^{2}}{n^{2}}\right)=o_{p}(\sqrt{p}),

because
(\sqrt{p}\,\log p)/n\to 0
and
(\sqrt{p}\,(\log p)^{2})/n^{2}\to 0
under
p=O(n^{\kappa})
with
\kappa<2
.

Next, define

R_{1n}^{\circ}:=-p^{2}n\sigma_{L}^{2}\sum_{j=1}^{p}W_{j}^{2}\Delta_{j}.

Since

\hat{C}_{j}^{2}-\sigma_{L}^{2}W_{j}^{2}=-2\sigma_{L}^{2}W_{j}\bar{Z}\bar{U}_{j}+\sigma_{L}^{2}\bar{Z}^{2}\bar{U}_{j}^{2},

Cauchy–Schwarz yields

\frac{1}{\sigma_{L}^{2}}\sum_{j=1}^{p}|\hat{C}_{j}^{2}-\sigma_{L}^{2}W_{j}^{2}|\leq 2|\bar{Z}|\,\|\bm{W}_{n}\|\,\|\bar{\bm{U}}\|+\bar{Z}^{2}\|\bar{\bm{U}}\|^{2}=O_{p}(n^{-3/2}).

Hence

\displaystyle\frac{|R_{1n}-R_{1n}^{\circ}|}{\sigma_{L}^{2}}

\displaystyle\leq p^{2}n\left(\max_{1\leq j\leq p}|\Delta_{j}|\right)\frac{1}{\sigma_{L}^{2}}\sum_{j=1}^{p}|\hat{C}_{j}^{2}-\sigma_{L}^{2}W_{j}^{2}|

\displaystyle=O_{p}\!\left(\frac{p\sqrt{\log p}}{n}\right)+O_{p}\!\left(\frac{p\log p}{n^{3/2}}\right)=o_{p}(\sqrt{p}).

Also,

\Delta_{j}=\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\eta_{ij}-\bar{U}_{j}^{2},\qquad\eta_{ij}=U_{ij}^{2}-\frac{1}{p}.

Therefore

R_{1n}^{\circ}=A_{n}+B_{n},

where

A_{n}:=-p^{2}\sigma_{L}^{2}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\sum_{j=1}^{p}W_{j}^{2}\eta_{ij},\qquad B_{n}:=np^{2}\sigma_{L}^{2}\sum_{j=1}^{p}W_{j}^{2}\bar{U}_{j}^{2}.

Using
\|\bm{W}_{n}\|^{2}=O_{p}(n^{-1})
and
\max_{j}\bar{U}_{j}^{2}=O_{p}((\log p)/(np))
, we get

\frac{|B_{n}|}{\sigma_{L}^{2}}\leq np^{2}\|\bm{W}_{n}\|^{2}\max_{1\leq j\leq p}\bar{U}_{j}^{2}=O_{p}\!\left(\frac{p\log p}{n}\right)=o_{p}(\sqrt{p}).

Now define

W_{j}^{(-i)}:=\frac{1}{n}\sum_{k\neq i}Z_{k}U_{kj},\qquad W_{j}=W_{j}^{(-i)}+\frac{1}{n}Z_{i}U_{ij}.

Expanding
W_{j}^{2}
gives
A_{n}=A_{1n}+A_{2n}+A_{3n}
, where

A_{1n}=-p^{2}\sigma_{L}^{2}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\sum_{j=1}^{p}\bigl(W_{j}^{(-i)}\bigr)^{2}\eta_{ij},

A_{2n}=-\frac{2p^{2}\sigma_{L}^{2}}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}Z_{i}\sum_{j=1}^{p}U_{ij}W_{j}^{(-i)}\eta_{ij},\qquad A_{3n}=-\frac{p^{2}\sigma_{L}^{2}}{n^{2}}\sum_{i=1}^{n}Z_{i}^{2}\sum_{j=1}^{p}U_{ij}^{2}\eta_{ij}.

Lemma
A.9
yields
A_{1n}/\sigma_{L}^{2}=o_{p}(\sqrt{p})
. For
A_{2n}
, write

A_{2n}=-\frac{2p^{2}\sigma_{L}^{2}}{n^{2}}\sum_{i\neq k}Z_{i}Z_{k}B_{ik},\qquad B_{ik}:=\sum_{j=1}^{p}U_{ij}U_{kj}\eta_{ij}.

Conditioning on
\bm{U}_{k}
, set
a_{j}=U_{kj}
and
V_{ij}=U_{ij}\eta_{ij}=U_{ij}^{3}-p^{-1}U_{ij}
. By spherical symmetry,
\mathbb{E}(V_{ij})=0
and
\mathbb{E}(V_{ij}V_{im})=0
for
j\neq m
. Using

\mathbb{E}(U_{ij}^{2})=\frac{1}{p},\qquad\mathbb{E}(U_{ij}^{4})=\frac{3}{p(p+2)},\qquad\mathbb{E}(U_{ij}^{6})=\frac{15}{p(p+2)(p+4)},

one gets
\mathbb{E}(V_{ij}^{2})=O(p^{-3})
, hence
\mathbb{E}(B_{ik}^{2}\mid\bm{U}_{k})\leq Cp^{-3}
and so
\mathbb{E}(B_{ik}^{2})=O(p^{-3})
. Expanding
\mathbb{E}\{(A_{2n}/\sigma_{L}^{2})^{2}\}
and using independence and centering of the
Z
’s shows that only index pairings with
\{r,s\}=\{i,k\}
survive, whence

\mathbb{E}\left\{\left(\frac{A_{2n}}{\sigma_{L}^{2}}\right)^{2}\right\}=O\!\left(\frac{p^{4}}{n^{4}}\cdot n^{2}\cdot p^{-3}\right)=O\!\left(\frac{p}{n^{2}}\right).

Thus
A_{2n}/\sigma_{L}^{2}=o_{p}(\sqrt{p})
. For
A_{3n}
, set

T_{i}:=\sum_{j=1}^{p}U_{ij}^{2}\eta_{ij}=\sum_{j=1}^{p}U_{ij}^{4}-\frac{1}{p}.

Since
\mathbb{E}\sum_{j=1}^{p}U_{ij}^{4}=3/(p+2)=O(p^{-1})
, we have
\mathbb{E}|T_{i}|=O(p^{-1})
. Therefore

\mathbb{E}\left|\frac{A_{3n}}{\sigma_{L}^{2}}\right|\leq\frac{p^{2}}{n^{2}}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\mathbb{E}(Z_{i}^{2})\mathbb{E}|T_{i}|=O\!\left(\frac{p}{n}\right),

so
A_{3n}/\sigma_{L}^{2}=o_{p}(\sqrt{p})
by Markov’s inequality. Hence
A_{n}/\sigma_{L}^{2}=o_{p}(\sqrt{p})
, and therefore

\frac{R_{1n}^{\circ}}{\sigma_{L}^{2}}=o_{p}(\sqrt{p}),\qquad\frac{R_{1n}}{\sigma_{L}^{2}}=o_{p}(\sqrt{p}).

Combining this with
R_{2n}/\sigma_{L}^{2}=o_{p}(\sqrt{p})
gives
R_{U,n}/\sigma_{L}^{2}=o_{p}(\sqrt{p})
.
∎

A.3.2
Proof of Theorem
3.2

Proof of Theorem
3.2
.

Recall the idealized statistic

\widetilde{T}_{\mathrm{sum}}=\frac{np}{\hat{\sigma}_{L}^{2}}\sum_{j=1}^{p}\hat{C}_{j}^{2}.

By definition,

T_{\mathrm{sum}}-\widetilde{T}_{\mathrm{sum}}=\frac{1}{\hat{\sigma}_{L}^{2}}\underbrace{n\sum_{j=1}^{p}\hat{C}_{j}^{2}\bigl(\hat{\sigma}_{U,j}^{-2}-p\bigr)}_{R_{U,n}}.

Lemma
A.10
shows that
R_{U,n}/\sigma_{L}^{2}=o_{p}(\sqrt{p})
. Since
\hat{\sigma}_{L}^{2}/\sigma_{L}^{2}=1+O_{p}(n^{-1/2})
,

T_{\mathrm{sum}}-\widetilde{T}_{\mathrm{sum}}=\frac{R_{U,n}/\sigma_{L}^{2}}{\hat{\sigma}_{L}^{2}/\sigma_{L}^{2}}=o_{p}(\sqrt{p}),\qquad\frac{T_{\mathrm{sum}}-\widetilde{T}_{\mathrm{sum}}}{\sqrt{2p}}\xrightarrow{p}0.

On the other hand, Proposition
A.1
gives

\frac{\widetilde{T}_{\mathrm{sum}}-p}{\sqrt{2p}}\xrightarrow{d}N(0,1).

Therefore

\frac{T_{\mathrm{sum}}-p}{\sqrt{2p}}=\frac{\widetilde{T}_{\mathrm{sum}}-p}{\sqrt{2p}}+\frac{T_{\mathrm{sum}}-\widetilde{T}_{\mathrm{sum}}}{\sqrt{2p}}\xrightarrow{d}N(0,1)

by Slutsky’s theorem.
∎

A.4
Proof of Theorem
3.3

A.4.1
Auxiliary lemmas

Lemma A.11
.

Let

\bm{S}_{n}:=\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\bm{\xi}_{i}=\left(\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\xi_{i1},\dots,\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\xi_{ip}\right)^{\top}.

Under the assumptions of Theorem
3.3
, let
M_{n}=\sqrt{n}\,\|\bm{g}_{n}^{\rm or}\|_{\infty}
. Then

M_{n}=\|\bm{S}_{n}\|_{\infty}+\varepsilon_{n},\qquad\varepsilon_{n}=o_{p}\bigl((\log p)^{-1/2}\bigr),

and

\|\bm{S}_{n}\|_{\infty}=O_{p}(\sqrt{\log p}).

Consequently,

T_{\max}=\|\bm{S}_{n}\|_{\infty}^{2}-2\log p+\log\log p+o_{p}(1).

(32)

Proof.

By Theorem
3.1
,

\sqrt{n}\,\hat{\gamma}_{j}=\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\xi_{ij}+\sqrt{n}\,r_{n,j},

so

M_{n}=\|\bm{S}_{n}\|_{\infty}+\varepsilon_{n},\qquad|\varepsilon_{n}|\leq\sqrt{n}\max_{1\leq j\leq p}|r_{n,j}|=O_{p}\!\left(\frac{\log p+\sqrt{\log p}}{\sqrt{n}}\right)=O_{p}\!\left(\frac{\log p}{\sqrt{n}}\right).

Moreover,

\frac{(\log p)/\sqrt{n}}{(\log p)^{-1/2}}=\left\{\frac{(\log p)^{3}}{n}\right\}^{1/2}\longrightarrow 0,

because
\log p=o(n^{1/5})
. Hence
\varepsilon_{n}=o_{p}((\log p)^{-1/2})
.

Next, conditional on
Z_{1},\ldots,Z_{n}
, for every
j
,

\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\xi_{ij}=\frac{\sqrt{p}}{\sqrt{n}}\sum_{i=1}^{n}Z_{i}U_{ij}.

The weighted spherical concentration inequality gives

\Pr\left(\left|\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\xi_{ij}\right|>t\,\bigg|\,Z_{1},\ldots,Z_{n}\right)\leq 2\exp\left(-\frac{ct^{2}}{V_{n}}\right),\qquad V_{n}=n^{-1}\sum_{i=1}^{n}Z_{i}^{2}.

Since
V_{n}=1+O_{p}(n^{-1/2})
, on the event
V_{n}\leq 2
,

\Pr\left(\|\bm{S}_{n}\|_{\infty}>t\mid Z_{1},\ldots,Z_{n}\right)\leq 2p\exp(-ct^{2}/2).

Taking
t=C\sqrt{\log p}
with
C
large enough gives

\mathbb{P}(\|\bm{S}_{n}\|_{\infty}>C\sqrt{\log p})\to 0,

whence
\|\bm{S}_{n}\|_{\infty}=O_{p}(\sqrt{\log p})
.

Finally,

M_{n}^{2}-\|\bm{S}_{n}\|_{\infty}^{2}=(M_{n}-\|\bm{S}_{n}\|_{\infty})(M_{n}+\|\bm{S}_{n}\|_{\infty}).

The first factor is
o_{p}((\log p)^{-1/2})
, while the second is
O_{p}(\sqrt{\log p})
. Therefore
M_{n}^{2}-\|\bm{S}_{n}\|_{\infty}^{2}=o_{p}(1)
, which proves (
32
).
∎

Proposition A.2
.

Let

\bm{S}_{n}=\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\bm{X}_{i}\in\mathbb{R}^{p},

where
\bm{X}_{1},\dots,\bm{X}_{n}
are independent centered random vectors with

\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\mathrm{Cov}(\bm{X}_{i})=\mathbf{\Sigma},\qquad\min_{1\leq j\leq p}\mathbf{\Sigma}_{jj}\geq b>0.

Assume that, for a deterministic
B_{n}\geq 1
,

\max_{1\leq i\leq n}\max_{1\leq j\leq p}\|X_{ij}\|_{\psi_{1}}\leq B_{n},\qquad\frac{B_{n}^{2}(\log p)^{5}}{n}\to 0.

Let
\bm{G}\sim N(0,\mathbf{\Sigma})
. Then

\sup_{A\in\mathcal{A}^{\mathrm{re}}}\bigl|\mathbb{P}(\bm{S}_{n}\in A)-\mathbb{P}(\bm{G}\in A)\bigr|\to 0,

where
\mathcal{A}^{\mathrm{re}}
denotes the class of all hyperrectangles in
\mathbb{R}^{p}
. In particular,

\sup_{t\in\mathbb{R}}\bigl|\mathbb{P}(\|\bm{S}_{n}\|_{\infty}\leq t)-\mathbb{P}(\|\bm{G}\|_{\infty}\leq t)\bigr|\to 0.

Proof.

Koike’s hyperrectangle Gaussian approximation bound gives

\sup_{A\in\mathcal{A}^{\mathrm{re}}}\bigl|\mathbb{P}(\bm{S}_{n}\in A)-\mathbb{P}(\bm{G}\in A)\bigr|\leq C\left\{\frac{B_{n}^{2}(\log p)^{5}}{n}\right\}^{c_{0}}

for numerical constants
C,c_{0}>0
depending only on
b

(
19
, Theorem 2.1)
. The right hand side converges to zero. The last assertion follows by taking
A=[-t,t]^{p}
.
∎

Lemma A.12
.

Under the assumptions of Theorem
3.3
, if

\bm{G}=(G_{1},\dots,G_{p})^{\top}\sim N(0,\mathbf{I}_{p}),

then

\sup_{t\in\mathbb{R}}\left|\mathbb{P}(\|\bm{S}_{n}\|_{\infty}\leq t)-\mathbb{P}(\|\bm{G}\|_{\infty}\leq t)\right|\longrightarrow 0.

(33)

Proof.

Let

V_{n}=\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}Z_{i}^{2},\qquad\mathcal{E}_{n}=\left\{\frac{1}{2}\leq V_{n}\leq 2,\\
\max_{1\leq i\leq n}|Z_{i}|\leq b_{n,p}\right\}.

Assumption
3.1
(ii) gives
\Pr(\mathcal{E}_{n})\to 1
. Conditional on
Z_{1},\ldots,Z_{n}
, define

\bm{X}_{i}=V_{n}^{-1/2}\sqrt{p}\,Z_{i}\bm{U}_{i}.

Then

\mathbb{E}(\bm{X}_{i}\mid Z_{1},\ldots,Z_{n})=\bm{0},\qquad\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\mathrm{Cov}(\bm{X}_{i}\mid Z_{1},\ldots,Z_{n})=\mathbf{I}_{p}.

On
\mathcal{E}_{n}
,

\max_{i,j}\|X_{ij}\|_{\psi_{1}\mid Z_{1},\ldots,Z_{n}}\leq C\max_{i}|Z_{i}|V_{n}^{-1/2}\leq Cb_{n,p},

because
\sqrt{p}\,U_{ij}
has a bounded sub-Gaussian, hence bounded sub-exponential, norm uniformly in
p
and
j
. Proposition
A.2
, applied conditionally, gives

\sup_{t\in\mathbb{R}}\left|\Pr\left(V_{n}^{-1/2}\|\bm{S}_{n}\|_{\infty}\leq t\mid Z_{1},\ldots,Z_{n}\right)-\Pr(\|\bm{G}\|_{\infty}\leq t)\right|\leq C\left\{\frac{b_{n,p}^{2}(\log p)^{5}}{n}\right\}^{c_{0}}

on
\mathcal{E}_{n}
. Therefore

\sup_{t}\left|\Pr\left(V_{n}^{-1/2}\|\bm{S}_{n}\|_{\infty}\leq t\right)-\Pr(\|\bm{G}\|_{\infty}\leq t)\right|\to 0.

It remains to remove the factor
V_{n}^{-1/2}
. Since
\mathbb{E}(V_{n}-1)^{2}=O(n^{-1})
,

|V_{n}^{1/2}-1|=O_{p}(n^{-1/2}).

The Gaussian anti-concentration bound for the maximum of
p
standard normals gives, for
0<\varepsilon<1/2
,

\sup_{t\in\mathbb{R}}\Pr\{t<\|\bm{G}\|_{\infty}\leq t(1+\varepsilon)\}\leq C\varepsilon\sqrt{\log p}.

With
\varepsilon_{n}=|V_{n}^{1/2}-1|
,
\varepsilon_{n}\sqrt{\log p}=O_{p}(\sqrt{\log p/n})=o_{p}(1)
, and hence

\sup_{t}\left|\Pr(\|\bm{S}_{n}\|_{\infty}\leq t)-\Pr(V_{n}^{-1/2}\|\bm{S}_{n}\|_{\infty}\leq t)\right|\to 0.

Combining the last two displays proves (
33
).
∎

Lemma A.13
.

Let
\bm{G}\sim N(0,\mathbf{I}_{p})
and define

u_{p}(x):=\sqrt{2\log p-\log\log p+x},\qquad x\in\mathbb{R}.

Then, for every fixed
x\in\mathbb{R}
,

\mathbb{P}\bigl(\|\bm{G}\|_{\infty}^{2}-2\log p+\log\log p\leq x\bigr)\longrightarrow\exp\!\bigl(-\pi^{-1/2}e^{-x/2}\bigr).

Proof.

Since
G_{1},\dots,G_{p}
are i.i.d.
N(0,1)
,

\mathbb{P}(\|\bm{G}\|_{\infty}\leq u)=\bigl(1-2\bar{\Phi}(u)\bigr)^{p},\qquad u>0,

where
\bar{\Phi}(u)=1-\Phi(u)
. Taking
u=u_{p}(x)
gives

\mathbb{P}\bigl(\|\bm{G}\|_{\infty}^{2}-2\log p+\log\log p\leq x\bigr)=\bigl(1-2\bar{\Phi}(u_{p}(x))\bigr)^{p}.

By Mills’ ratio,

\bar{\Phi}(u)=\frac{\phi(u)}{u}\{1+o(1)\},\qquad\phi(u)=\frac{1}{\sqrt{2\pi}}e^{-u^{2}/2},\qquad u\to\infty.

Since
u_{p}(x)^{2}=2\log p-\log\log p+x
,

e^{-u_{p}(x)^{2}/2}=p^{-1}(\log p)^{1/2}e^{-x/2},\qquad u_{p}(x)\sim\sqrt{2\log p}.

Hence

2p\bar{\Phi}(u_{p}(x))\sim 2p\frac{\phi(u_{p}(x))}{u_{p}(x)}=2\cdot\frac{1}{\sqrt{2\pi}}\cdot\frac{(\log p)^{1/2}}{\sqrt{2\log p}}e^{-x/2}=\pi^{-1/2}e^{-x/2}.

Therefore
a_{p}:=2\bar{\Phi}(u_{p}(x))\to 0
and
pa_{p}\to\pi^{-1/2}e^{-x/2}
, so
(1-a_{p})^{p}\to\exp(-\pi^{-1/2}e^{-x/2})
.
∎

A.4.2
Proof of Theorem
3.3

Proof of Theorem
3.3
.

Lemma
A.11
gives

T_{\max}=\|\bm{S}_{n}\|_{\infty}^{2}-2\log p+\log\log p+o_{p}(1).

Fix
x\in\mathbb{R}
and set
u=u_{p}(x)
. By Lemma
A.12
,

\left|\mathbb{P}(\|\bm{S}_{n}\|_{\infty}\leq u)-\mathbb{P}(\|\bm{G}\|_{\infty}\leq u)\right|\to 0.

Equivalently,

\mathbb{P}\bigl(\|\bm{S}_{n}\|_{\infty}^{2}-2\log p+\log\log p\leq x\bigr)-\mathbb{P}\bigl(\|\bm{G}\|_{\infty}^{2}-2\log p+\log\log p\leq x\bigr)\to 0.

Lemma
A.13
now yields

\mathbb{P}\bigl(\|\bm{S}_{n}\|_{\infty}^{2}-2\log p+\log\log p\leq x\bigr)\longrightarrow\exp\!\bigl(-\pi^{-1/2}e^{-x/2}\bigr).

Combining this with the reduction above and applying Slutsky’s theorem proves

\mathbb{P}(T_{\max}\leq x)\longrightarrow\exp\!\bigl(-\pi^{-1/2}e^{-x/2}\bigr),\qquad x\in\mathbb{R}.

∎

A.5
Proof of Theorem
3.4

A.5.1
Auxiliary lemmas

Lemma A.14
.

Under Assumption
3.1
, and suppose that

p\to\infty,\qquad p=O(n^{\kappa})\quad\text{for some }\kappa\in(0,2).

Then

\frac{T_{\mathrm{sum}}-p}{\sqrt{2p}}=\frac{H_{n}}{\sqrt{2p}}+o_{p}(1).

Proof.

By Lemma
A.10
and the identity

T_{\mathrm{sum}}-\widetilde{T}_{\mathrm{sum}}=\hat{\sigma}_{L}^{-2}R_{U,n},

used in the proof of Theorem
3.2
, we have

T_{\mathrm{sum}}-\widetilde{T}_{\mathrm{sum}}=o_{p}(\sqrt{p}),

and by Lemma
A.4
,

\widetilde{T}_{\mathrm{sum}}-\widetilde{T}_{\mathrm{sum}}^{\circ}=o_{p}(\sqrt{p}).

Hence

T_{\mathrm{sum}}-\widetilde{T}_{\mathrm{sum}}^{\circ}=o_{p}(\sqrt{p}),\qquad\frac{T_{\mathrm{sum}}-\widetilde{T}_{\mathrm{sum}}^{\circ}}{\sqrt{2p}}=o_{p}(1).

Next, by Lemma
A.5
,

\widetilde{T}_{\mathrm{sum}}^{\circ}-p=\frac{p\bar{Z}^{2}+H_{n}}{1+A_{n}-\bar{Z}^{2}}.

Therefore,

\frac{\widetilde{T}_{\mathrm{sum}}^{\circ}-p}{\sqrt{2p}}=\frac{H_{n}}{\sqrt{2p}}\cdot\frac{1}{1+A_{n}-\bar{Z}^{2}}+\frac{p\bar{Z}^{2}}{\sqrt{2p}}\cdot\frac{1}{1+A_{n}-\bar{Z}^{2}}.

By Lemma
A.5
,

\frac{p\bar{Z}^{2}}{\sqrt{2p}}=o_{p}(1),\qquad 1+A_{n}-\bar{Z}^{2}\xrightarrow{p}1.

Since
H_{n}/\sqrt{2p}=O_{p}(1)
by Lemma
A.6
, Slutsky’s theorem gives

\frac{\widetilde{T}_{\mathrm{sum}}^{\circ}-p}{\sqrt{2p}}=\frac{H_{n}}{\sqrt{2p}}+o_{p}(1).

Combining the two reductions yields the claim.
∎

Lemma A.15
.

Under Assumption
3.1
,
p\to\infty
, and
p=O(n^{\kappa})
for some
\kappa\in(0,2)
. Fix an integer
d\geq 1
, and let
1\leq j_{1}<\cdots<j_{d}\leq p
be distinct indices. Then

\left(\frac{H_{n}}{\sqrt{2p}},\ S_{n,j_{1}},\dots,S_{n,j_{d}}\right)\xrightarrow{d}(N_{0},N_{1},\dots,N_{d}),

where
N_{0}\sim N(0,1)
,
(N_{1},\dots,N_{d})\sim N(0,\mathbf{I}_{d})
, and
N_{0}
is independent of
(N_{1},\dots,N_{d})
.

Proof.

Let

\bm{S}_{n,J}:=(S_{n,j_{1}},\dots,S_{n,j_{d}})^{\top},\qquad T_{n}(\bm{a}):=\frac{H_{n}}{\sqrt{2p}}+\bm{a}^{\top}\bm{S}_{n,J},\qquad\bm{a}\in\mathbb{R}^{d}.

We prove that for every fixed
\bm{a}\in\mathbb{R}^{d}
,

T_{n}(\bm{a})\xrightarrow{d}N\bigl(0,1+\|\bm{a}\|_{2}^{2}\bigr).

The Cramér–Wold device then yields the stated joint convergence.

Recall from the proof of Lemma
A.6
that

H_{n}=\sum_{k=2}^{n}M_{n,k},\qquad M_{n,k}:=\frac{2}{n}\bm{R}_{k-1}^{\top}\bm{\xi}_{k},\qquad\bm{R}_{k-1}:=\sum_{i=1}^{k-1}\bm{\xi}_{i},\qquad\mathcal{F}_{k}:=\sigma(\bm{\xi}_{1},\dots,\bm{\xi}_{k}).

Since

\bm{a}^{\top}\bm{S}_{n,J}=\sum_{k=1}^{n}\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}\bm{a}^{\top}\bm{\xi}_{k,J},\qquad\bm{\xi}_{k,J}:=(\xi_{kj_{1}},\dots,\xi_{kj_{d}})^{\top},

we may write

T_{n}(\bm{a})=\sum_{k=1}^{n}D_{n,k}(\bm{a}),

where

D_{n,k}(\bm{a}):=\frac{2}{n\sqrt{2p}}\bm{R}_{k-1}^{\top}\bm{\xi}_{k}+\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}\bm{a}^{\top}\bm{\xi}_{k,J},\qquad k=1,\dots,n,

with the convention
\bm{R}_{0}=\bm{0}
. Since
\mathbb{E}(\bm{\xi}_{k}\mid\mathcal{F}_{k-1})=0
,
\{D_{n,k}(\bm{a}),\mathcal{F}_{k}\}
is a martingale difference array.

Let

V_{n}(\bm{a}):=\sum_{k=1}^{n}\mathbb{E}\bigl(D_{n,k}(\bm{a})^{2}\mid\mathcal{F}_{k-1}\bigr)=V_{n,1}+V_{n,2}+V_{n,3},

where

V_{n,1}:=\sum_{k=1}^{n}\mathbb{E}\left\{\left(\frac{2}{n\sqrt{2p}}\bm{R}_{k-1}^{\top}\bm{\xi}_{k}\right)^{2}\Bigm|\mathcal{F}_{k-1}\right\},

V_{n,2}:=\sum_{k=1}^{n}\mathbb{E}\left\{\left(\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}\bm{a}^{\top}\bm{\xi}_{k,J}\right)^{2}\Bigm|\mathcal{F}_{k-1}\right\},

V_{n,3}:=2\sum_{k=1}^{n}\mathbb{E}\left\{\frac{2}{n\sqrt{2p}}\bm{R}_{k-1}^{\top}\bm{\xi}_{k}\cdot\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}\bm{a}^{\top}\bm{\xi}_{k,J}\Bigm|\mathcal{F}_{k-1}\right\}.

The first term is exactly the predictable quadratic variation from Lemma
A.6
, so

V_{n,1}\xrightarrow{p}1.

For the second term, since
\mathbb{E}(\bm{\xi}_{i}\bm{\xi}_{i}^{\top})=\mathbf{I}_{p}
, we have

V_{n,2}=\sum_{k=1}^{n}\frac{1}{n}\,\bm{a}^{\top}\mathbf{I}_{d}\bm{a}=\|\bm{a}\|_{2}^{2}.

For the cross term,

V_{n,3}=\frac{4}{n^{3/2}\sqrt{2p}}\sum_{k=2}^{n}\bm{a}^{\top}\bm{R}_{k-1,J},\qquad\bm{R}_{k-1,J}:=(R_{k-1,j_{1}},\dots,R_{k-1,j_{d}})^{\top}.

Now, for each fixed
j
,

\sum_{k=2}^{n}R_{k-1,j}=\sum_{i=1}^{n-1}(n-i)\xi_{ij},

whence

\mathbb{E}\left(\sum_{k=2}^{n}R_{k-1,j}\right)^{2}=\sum_{i=1}^{n-1}(n-i)^{2}\mathbb{E}(\xi_{ij}^{2})=O(n^{3}).

Since
d
is fixed, it follows that

\mathbb{E}(V_{n,3}^{2})=O\!\left(\frac{1}{p}\right)\to 0,\qquad V_{n,3}=o_{p}(1).

Therefore

V_{n}(\bm{a})\xrightarrow{p}1+\|\bm{a}\|_{2}^{2}.

It remains to verify the Lindeberg condition. By
|x+y|^{4}\leq 8|x|^{4}+8|y|^{4}
,

\sum_{k=1}^{n}\mathbb{E}|D_{n,k}(\bm{a})|^{4}\lesssim\sum_{k=1}^{n}\mathbb{E}\left|\frac{2}{n\sqrt{2p}}\bm{R}_{k-1}^{\top}\bm{\xi}_{k}\right|^{4}+\sum_{k=1}^{n}\mathbb{E}\left|\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}\bm{a}^{\top}\bm{\xi}_{k,J}\right|^{4}.

The first term is
o(1)
by the fourth-moment bound already established at the end of the proof of Lemma
A.6
. Since
d
is fixed and the coordinates
\xi_{ij}
have uniformly bounded fourth moments, the second term is bounded by

Cn\cdot n^{-2}=O(n^{-1})\to 0.

Thus the martingale Lindeberg condition holds. Hall–Heyde’s martingale CLT
(
14
, Corollary 3.1)
gives

T_{n}(\bm{a})\xrightarrow{d}N\bigl(0,1+\|\bm{a}\|_{2}^{2}\bigr).

The desired joint convergence now follows by the Cramér–Wold device.
∎

Lemma A.16
.

Fix an integer
d\geq 1
and a coordinate set
J=\{j_{1},\dots,j_{d}\}\subset\{1,\dots,p\}
with
|J|=d
. Then for each
i
there exist random objects

B_{i}\sim\mathrm{Beta}(d/2,(p-d)/2),\qquad\bm{V}_{i}\sim\mathrm{Unif}(\mathbb{S}^{d-1}),\qquad\bm{W}_{i}\sim\mathrm{Unif}(\mathbb{S}^{p-d-1}),

independent of each other and independent of
Z_{i}
, such that

\bm{U}_{i,J}=\sqrt{B_{i}}\,\bm{V}_{i},\qquad\bm{U}_{i,J^{c}}=\sqrt{1-B_{i}}\,\bm{W}_{i}.

Consequently, if we set

a_{i}:=\sqrt{p}\,Z_{i}\sqrt{B_{i}},\qquad b_{i}:=\sqrt{p}\,Z_{i}\sqrt{1-B_{i}},

then

\bm{S}_{n,J}:=(S_{n,j_{1}},\dots,S_{n,j_{d}})^{\top}=\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}\sum_{i=1}^{n}a_{i}\bm{V}_{i},

and

H_{n}=H_{n,J}+H_{n,J^{c}},\qquad H_{n,J}:=\frac{2}{n}\sum_{1\leq i<k\leq n}a_{i}a_{k}\bm{V}_{i}^{\top}\bm{V}_{k},\qquad H_{n,J^{c}}:=\frac{2}{n}\sum_{1\leq i<k\leq n}b_{i}b_{k}\bm{W}_{i}^{\top}\bm{W}_{k}.

Moreover, with

\mathcal{G}_{n}:=\sigma\{(Z_{i},B_{i}):1\leq i\leq n\},

the random vector
\bm{S}_{n,J}
and the scalar
H_{n,J^{c}}
are conditionally independent given
\mathcal{G}_{n}
.

Proof.

Let
\bm{G}_{i}=(\bm{G}_{i,J}^{\top},\bm{G}_{i,J^{c}}^{\top})^{\top}\sim N_{p}(0,\mathbf{I}_{p})
, and write

\bm{U}_{i}=\frac{\bm{G}_{i}}{\|\bm{G}_{i}\|}=\left(\frac{\bm{G}_{i,J}}{\|\bm{G}_{i}\|},\frac{\bm{G}_{i,J^{c}}}{\|\bm{G}_{i}\|}\right).

Set

B_{i}:=\frac{\|\bm{G}_{i,J}\|^{2}}{\|\bm{G}_{i}\|^{2}},\qquad\bm{V}_{i}:=\frac{\bm{G}_{i,J}}{\|\bm{G}_{i,J}\|},\qquad\bm{W}_{i}:=\frac{\bm{G}_{i,J^{c}}}{\|\bm{G}_{i,J^{c}}\|}.

Since
\|\bm{G}_{i,J}\|^{2}\sim\chi_{d}^{2}
,
\|\bm{G}_{i,J^{c}}\|^{2}\sim\chi_{p-d}^{2}
, and these two random variables are independent, we have

B_{i}\sim\mathrm{Beta}(d/2,(p-d)/2).

Moreover,
\bm{V}_{i}\sim\mathrm{Unif}(\mathbb{S}^{d-1})
,
\bm{W}_{i}\sim\mathrm{Unif}(\mathbb{S}^{p-d-1})
, and
B_{i}
,
\bm{V}_{i}
, and
\bm{W}_{i}
are mutually independent. Therefore

\bm{U}_{i,J}=\sqrt{B_{i}}\,\bm{V}_{i},\qquad\bm{U}_{i,J^{c}}=\sqrt{1-B_{i}}\,\bm{W}_{i}.

Multiplying by
\sqrt{p}\,Z_{i}
yields the displayed representations for
\bm{S}_{n,J}
,
H_{n,J}
, and
H_{n,J^{c}}
. Finally, conditional on

\mathcal{G}_{n}=\sigma\{(Z_{i},B_{i}):1\leq i\leq n\},

the vectors
\bm{V}_{1},\dots,\bm{V}_{n}
and
\bm{W}_{1},\dots,\bm{W}_{n}
are independent, and hence
\bm{S}_{n,J}
and
H_{n,J^{c}}
are conditionally independent.
∎

Lemma A.17
.

Fix
d\geq 1
and
J=\{j_{1},\dots,j_{d}\}
. Assume that
p\to\infty
,
p=O(n^{\kappa})
for some fixed
\kappa>0
, and
\log p=o(n^{1/5})
. Let

\Delta_{n,J}:=\frac{H_{n,J}}{\sqrt{2p}}.

Then there exists a constant
C_{0}>0
such that, with

\delta_{n}:=\frac{C_{0}\log p}{\sqrt{p}},

we have
\delta_{n}\to 0
and

\mathbb{P}\bigl(|\Delta_{n,J}|>\delta_{n}\bigr)=o\!\left(\{2\bar{\Phi}(u_{p}(y))\}^{d}\right)\qquad(n\to\infty),

for every fixed
y\in\mathbb{R}
.

Proof.

By the identity

\Bigl\|\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}\sum_{i=1}^{n}a_{i}\bm{V}_{i}\Bigr\|^{2}=\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}a_{i}^{2}+\frac{2}{n}\sum_{1\leq i<k\leq n}a_{i}a_{k}\bm{V}_{i}^{\top}\bm{V}_{k},

we may write

H_{n,J}=\|\bm{S}_{n,J}\|_{2}^{2}-\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}a_{i}^{2}.

Since
d
is fixed and
B_{i}\sim\mathrm{Beta}(d/2,(p-d)/2)
,
\sup_{p}\mathbb{E}(pB_{i})^{m}<\infty
for every fixed
m
. Assumption
3.1
(ii) gives
\sup_{p}\mathbb{E}|Z_{i}|^{8+\eta_{Z}}<\infty
, hence

\mathbb{E}(a_{i}^{2})=p\mathbb{E}(Z_{i}^{2})\mathbb{E}(B_{i})=d,\qquad\sup_{p}\mathbb{E}(a_{i}^{4})<\infty,\qquad\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}a_{i}^{2}=O_{p}(1).

Moreover, the beta–gamma representation of
B_{i}
gives

\max_{1\leq i\leq n}pB_{i}=O_{p}(\log n),\qquad\max_{1\leq i\leq n}|a_{i}|=O_{p}\{b_{n,p}\sqrt{\log n}\}.

Thus

\frac{\sqrt{\log p}\max_{i}|a_{i}|}{\sqrt{\sum_{i=1}^{n}a_{i}^{2}}}=O_{p}\left(\sqrt{\frac{b_{n,p}^{2}\log p\log n}{n}}\right)=o_{p}(1).

Conditional on
\mathcal{G}_{n}
,
\bm{S}_{n,J}=n^{-1/2}\sum_{i}a_{i}\bm{V}_{i}
is a fixed-dimensional sum of independent bounded spherical variables. Bernstein’s inequality applied on the above coefficient events gives that, for every fixed
M>0
, there is a sufficiently large
C_{0}
such that

\mathbb{P}(|H_{n,J}|>C_{0}\log p)=O(p^{-M}).

Here the polynomial relation between
p
and
n
is used to express the coefficient-event probability on the
p^{-M}
scale. Since

2\bar{\Phi}(u_{p}(y))\asymp p^{-1},

we have

\{2\bar{\Phi}(u_{p}(y))\}^{d}\asymp p^{-d}.

Choosing
M>d+1
proves the claim.
∎

Lemma A.18
.

Fix an integer
d\geq 1
, let
J=\{j_{1},\dots,j_{d}\}\subset\{1,\dots,p\}
with
|J|=d
, and write

q:=p-d,\qquad Q_{n,J}:=\frac{H_{n,J^{c}}}{\sqrt{2p}}.

Let

\mathcal{G}_{n}:=\sigma\{(Z_{i},B_{i}):1\leq i\leq n\},

where
B_{i}\sim\mathrm{Beta}(d/2,q/2)
is the block-mass variable in Lemma
A.16
. Under Assumption
3.1
, if
p\to\infty
as
n\to\infty
,

\sup_{t\in\mathbb{R}}\Bigl|\mathbb{P}\bigl(Q_{n,J}\leq t\mid\mathcal{G}_{n}\bigr)-\Phi(t)\Bigr|\longrightarrow 0\qquad\text{in }L^{1}.

Consequently, for every fixed
x\in\mathbb{R}
and every deterministic sequence
\delta_{n}\downarrow 0
,

\sup_{|t-x|\leq\delta_{n}}\Bigl|\mathbb{P}\bigl(Q_{n,J}\leq t\mid\mathcal{G}_{n}\bigr)-\Phi(t)\Bigr|\longrightarrow 0\qquad\text{in }L^{1}.

Proof.

Condition on
\mathcal{G}_{n}
and set

b_{i}:=\sqrt{p}\,Z_{i}\sqrt{1-B_{i}},\qquad\bm{\eta}_{i}:=b_{i}\bm{W}_{i}\in\mathbb{R}^{q},\qquad i=1,\dots,n,

where
\bm{W}_{i}\sim\mathrm{Unif}(\mathbb{S}^{q-1})
are i.i.d. and independent of
\mathcal{G}_{n}
. Then, conditionally on
\mathcal{G}_{n}
, the vectors
\bm{\eta}_{1},\dots,\bm{\eta}_{n}
are independent, centered, and satisfy

\mathbb{E}(\bm{\eta}_{i}\mid\mathcal{G}_{n})=0,\qquad\mathbb{E}(\bm{\eta}_{i}\bm{\eta}_{i}^{\top}\mid\mathcal{G}_{n})=\frac{b_{i}^{2}}{q}\mathbf{I}_{q},\qquad\|\bm{\eta}_{i}\|^{2}=b_{i}^{2}.

By Lemma
A.16
,

H_{n,J^{c}}=\frac{2}{n}\sum_{1\leq i<k\leq n}\bm{\eta}_{i}^{\top}\bm{\eta}_{k}.

Define the conditional filtration

\mathcal{H}_{n,k}:=\sigma(\bm{W}_{1},\dots,\bm{W}_{k})\vee\mathcal{G}_{n},\qquad k=0,1,\dots,n,

and the conditional partial sums

\bm{R}_{k}^{\circ}:=\sum_{i=1}^{k}\bm{\eta}_{i}.

Then

H_{n,J^{c}}=\sum_{k=2}^{n}M_{n,k}^{\circ},\qquad M_{n,k}^{\circ}:=\frac{2}{n}(\bm{R}_{k-1}^{\circ})^{\top}\bm{\eta}_{k},

so that

Q_{n,J}=\sum_{k=2}^{n}D_{n,k}^{\circ},\qquad D_{n,k}^{\circ}:=\frac{M_{n,k}^{\circ}}{\sqrt{2p}}.

Since
\bm{\eta}_{k}
is independent of
\mathcal{H}_{n,k-1}
and centered conditional on
\mathcal{G}_{n}
, the array

\{D_{n,k}^{\circ},\mathcal{H}_{n,k}\}_{k=2}^{n}

is a martingale difference array under the conditional law given
\mathcal{G}_{n}
.

We now apply a quantitative martingale central limit theorem. Let

V_{n,J}^{\circ}:=\sum_{k=2}^{n}\mathbb{E}\bigl\{(D_{n,k}^{\circ})^{2}\mid\mathcal{H}_{n,k-1}\bigr\}

be the predictable quadratic variation. By Haeusler’s martingale Berry–Esseen bound
13
, there exists a universal constant
C>0
such that, conditionally on
\mathcal{G}_{n}
,

\sup_{t\in\mathbb{R}}\Bigl|\mathbb{P}\bigl(Q_{n,J}\leq t\mid\mathcal{G}_{n}\bigr)-\Phi(t)\Bigr|\leq C\Biggl(\mathbb{E}\bigl\{(V_{n,J}^{\circ}-1)^{2}\mid\mathcal{G}_{n}\bigr\}+\sum_{k=2}^{n}\mathbb{E}\bigl\{|D_{n,k}^{\circ}|^{4}\mid\mathcal{G}_{n}\bigr\}\Biggr)^{1/5}.

(34)

Therefore it is enough to prove

\mathbb{E}\bigl(V_{n,J}^{\circ}-1\bigr)^{2}\to 0,\qquad\mathbb{E}\sum_{k=2}^{n}\mathbb{E}\bigl\{|D_{n,k}^{\circ}|^{4}\mid\mathcal{G}_{n}\bigr\}\to 0.

(35)

If (
35
) holds, Jensen’s inequality gives

\mathbb{E}\sup_{t\in\mathbb{R}}\Bigl|\mathbb{P}\bigl(Q_{n,J}\leq t\mid\mathcal{G}_{n}\bigr)-\Phi(t)\Bigr|\leq C\Biggl(\mathbb{E}\bigl(V_{n,J}^{\circ}-1\bigr)^{2}+\mathbb{E}\sum_{k=2}^{n}\mathbb{E}\bigl\{|D_{n,k}^{\circ}|^{4}\mid\mathcal{G}_{n}\bigr\}\Biggr)^{1/5}\to 0.

We first study
V_{n,J}^{\circ}
. Since

\mathbb{E}\bigl\{\bm{\eta}_{k}\bm{\eta}_{k}^{\top}\mid\mathcal{G}_{n}\bigr\}=\frac{b_{k}^{2}}{q}\mathbf{I}_{q},

we have

V_{n,J}^{\circ}=\frac{2}{pn^{2}q}\sum_{k=2}^{n}b_{k}^{2}\,\|\bm{R}_{k-1}^{\circ}\|^{2}.

Write

V_{n,J}^{\circ}=A_{n,J}^{\circ}+B_{n,J}^{\circ},

where

A_{n,J}^{\circ}:=\frac{2}{pn^{2}q}\sum_{k=2}^{n}b_{k}^{2}\sum_{i=1}^{k-1}b_{i}^{2},\qquad B_{n,J}^{\circ}:=\frac{4}{pn^{2}q}\sum_{k=2}^{n}b_{k}^{2}\sum_{1\leq i<j\leq k-1}\bm{\eta}_{i}^{\top}\bm{\eta}_{j}.

Since
\sum_{k=2}^{n}\sum_{i=1}^{k-1}b_{i}^{2}b_{k}^{2}=\frac{1}{2}\{(\sum_{i=1}^{n}b_{i}^{2})^{2}-\sum_{i=1}^{n}b_{i}^{4}\}
,

A_{n,J}^{\circ}=\frac{1}{pn^{2}q}\left\{\Bigl(\sum_{i=1}^{n}b_{i}^{2}\Bigr)^{2}-\sum_{i=1}^{n}b_{i}^{4}\right\}.

Let

T_{i}:=\frac{b_{i}^{2}}{p}=Z_{i}^{2}(1-B_{i}).

Because
d
is fixed,
\sup_{n,p}\mathbb{E}(1-B_{i})^{m}<\infty
for every fixed integer
m\geq 1
. Assumption
3.1
(ii) gives

\sup_{n,p}\mathbb{E}|T_{i}|^{4+\eta_{Z}/2}<\infty.

Also,

\mu_{n}:=\mathbb{E}(T_{i})=\mathbb{E}(Z_{i}^{2})\mathbb{E}(1-B_{i})=\frac{q}{p}=1-\frac{d}{p}.

Hence

A_{n,J}^{\circ}=\frac{p}{q}\left\{\bar{T}_{n}^{\,2}-\frac{1}{n^{2}}\sum_{i=1}^{n}T_{i}^{2}\right\},\qquad\bar{T}_{n}:=\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}T_{i}.

Since
\sup_{n,p}\mathbb{E}|T_{i}|^{4}<\infty
, we have

\mathbb{E}(\bar{T}_{n}-\mu_{n})^{4}=O(n^{-2}),\qquad\mathbb{E}\left(\frac{1}{n^{2}}\sum_{i=1}^{n}T_{i}^{2}\right)^{2}=O(n^{-2}).

Using

\bar{T}_{n}^{\,2}-\mu_{n}^{2}=(\bar{T}_{n}-\mu_{n})(\bar{T}_{n}+\mu_{n})

and the bounded fourth moments of
\bar{T}_{n}
and
\mu_{n}
, it follows that

\mathbb{E}\bigl(\bar{T}_{n}^{\,2}-\mu_{n}^{2}\bigr)^{2}=O(n^{-1}).

Since
\mu_{n}=q/p
, we conclude that

\mathbb{E}\bigl(A_{n,J}^{\circ}-1\bigr)^{2}=O(n^{-1}+p^{-2})=o(1).

Next, we bound
B_{n,J}^{\circ}
. Observe that

\sum_{k=2}^{n}b_{k}^{2}\sum_{1\leq i<j\leq k-1}\bm{\eta}_{i}^{\top}\bm{\eta}_{j}=\sum_{1\leq i<j\leq n}\Bigl(\sum_{k=j+1}^{n}b_{k}^{2}\Bigr)\bm{\eta}_{i}^{\top}\bm{\eta}_{j}.

Therefore, conditionally on
\mathcal{G}_{n}
,

B_{n,J}^{\circ}=\frac{4}{pn^{2}q}\sum_{1\leq i<j\leq n}c_{ij}\,\bm{W}_{i}^{\top}\bm{W}_{j},\qquad c_{ij}:=b_{i}b_{j}\sum_{k=j+1}^{n}b_{k}^{2}.

Because
\mathbb{E}(\bm{W}_{i}^{\top}\bm{W}_{j}\mid\mathcal{G}_{n})=0
for
i\neq j
, and because mixed products vanish unless the unordered pairs coincide,

\mathbb{E}\bigl\{(B_{n,J}^{\circ})^{2}\mid\mathcal{G}_{n}\bigr\}\leq\frac{C}{p^{2}n^{4}q^{2}}\sum_{1\leq i<j\leq n}c_{ij}^{2}\,\mathbb{E}\bigl\{(\bm{W}_{i}^{\top}\bm{W}_{j})^{2}\mid\mathcal{G}_{n}\bigr\}.

For independent uniform directions on
\mathbb{S}^{q-1}
,

\mathbb{E}\bigl\{(\bm{W}_{i}^{\top}\bm{W}_{j})^{2}\mid\mathcal{G}_{n}\bigr\}=\frac{1}{q}.

Hence

\mathbb{E}\bigl\{(B_{n,J}^{\circ})^{2}\mid\mathcal{G}_{n}\bigr\}\leq\frac{C}{p^{2}n^{4}q^{3}}\sum_{1\leq i<j\leq n}c_{ij}^{2}.

Since

c_{ij}^{2}\leq b_{i}^{2}b_{j}^{2}\Bigl(\sum_{k=1}^{n}b_{k}^{2}\Bigr)^{2},

we obtain

\sum_{1\leq i<j\leq n}c_{ij}^{2}\leq\Bigl(\sum_{i=1}^{n}b_{i}^{2}\Bigr)^{4}.

Thus

\mathbb{E}(B_{n,J}^{\circ})^{2}\leq\frac{C}{p^{2}n^{4}q^{3}}\,\mathbb{E}\Bigl(\sum_{i=1}^{n}b_{i}^{2}\Bigr)^{4}.

Now
b_{i}^{2}=pT_{i}
and
\sup_{n,p}\mathbb{E}|T_{i}|^{4}<\infty
, so

\mathbb{E}\Bigl(\sum_{i=1}^{n}b_{i}^{2}\Bigr)^{4}=O(n^{4}p^{4}).

Consequently,

\mathbb{E}(B_{n,J}^{\circ})^{2}=O(p^{-1})=o(1).

Combining the bounds for
A_{n,J}^{\circ}
and
B_{n,J}^{\circ}
yields

\mathbb{E}\bigl(V_{n,J}^{\circ}-1\bigr)^{2}\leq 2\mathbb{E}\bigl(A_{n,J}^{\circ}-1\bigr)^{2}+2\mathbb{E}(B_{n,J}^{\circ})^{2}=o(1).

This proves the first part of (
35
).

We turn to the fourth-moment term. Since
\bm{\eta}_{k}=b_{k}\bm{W}_{k}
, for every deterministic
\bm{a}\in\mathbb{R}^{q}
,

\mathbb{E}\bigl\{(\bm{a}^{\top}\bm{\eta}_{k})^{4}\mid\mathcal{G}_{n}\bigr\}=b_{k}^{4}\mathbb{E}(\bm{a}^{\top}\bm{W}_{k})^{4}=\frac{3b_{k}^{4}}{q(q+2)}\|\bm{a}\|^{4}.

Hence

\mathbb{E}\bigl\{(M_{n,k}^{\circ})^{4}\mid\mathcal{G}_{n}\bigr\}\leq\frac{Cb_{k}^{4}}{n^{4}q^{2}}\,\mathbb{E}\bigl(\|\bm{R}_{k-1}^{\circ}\|^{4}\mid\mathcal{G}_{n}\bigr).

Now

\bm{R}_{k-1}^{\circ}=\sum_{i=1}^{k-1}\bm{\eta}_{i},\qquad\|\bm{\eta}_{i}\|^{2}=b_{i}^{2},\qquad\mathbb{E}(\bm{\eta}_{i}^{\top}\bm{\eta}_{j})^{2}\mid\mathcal{G}_{n}=\frac{b_{i}^{2}b_{j}^{2}}{q}\leq b_{i}^{2}b_{j}^{2}.

Expanding
\|\bm{R}_{k-1}^{\circ}\|^{4}
and using independence gives

\mathbb{E}\bigl(\|\bm{R}_{k-1}^{\circ}\|^{4}\mid\mathcal{G}_{n}\bigr)\leq C\Bigl(\sum_{i=1}^{k-1}b_{i}^{2}\Bigr)^{2}.

Therefore

\sum_{k=2}^{n}\mathbb{E}\bigl\{|D_{n,k}^{\circ}|^{4}\mid\mathcal{G}_{n}\bigr\}\leq\frac{C}{p^{2}n^{4}q^{2}}\sum_{k=2}^{n}b_{k}^{4}\Bigl(\sum_{i=1}^{k-1}b_{i}^{2}\Bigr)^{2}\leq\frac{C}{p^{2}n^{4}q^{2}}\Bigl(\sum_{k=1}^{n}b_{k}^{4}\Bigr)\Bigl(\sum_{i=1}^{n}b_{i}^{2}\Bigr)^{2}.

Taking expectations and applying Cauchy–Schwarz,

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\sum_{k=2}^{n}\mathbb{E}\bigl\{|D_{n,k}^{\circ}|^{4}\mid\mathcal{G}_{n}\bigr\}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{C}{p^{2}n^{4}q^{2}}\Bigl\{\mathbb{E}\Bigl(\sum_{k=1}^{n}b_{k}^{4}\Bigr)^{2}\Bigr\}^{1/2}\Bigl\{\mathbb{E}\Bigl(\sum_{i=1}^{n}b_{i}^{2}\Bigr)^{4}\Bigr\}^{1/2}

\displaystyle=\frac{C}{p^{2}n^{4}q^{2}}\cdot O(np^{2})\cdot O(n^{2}p^{2})=O(n^{-1})=o(1).

Here we used
\sup_{n,p}\mathbb{E}|T_{i}|^{8}<\infty
, which implies

\mathbb{E}\Bigl(\sum_{k=1}^{n}b_{k}^{4}\Bigr)^{2}=O(n^{2}p^{4}),\qquad\mathbb{E}\Bigl(\sum_{i=1}^{n}b_{i}^{2}\Bigr)^{4}=O(n^{4}p^{4}).

This proves the second part of (
35
).

We have therefore shown that the right-hand side of (
34
) tends to zero in
L^{1}
, and hence

\sup_{t\in\mathbb{R}}\Bigl|\mathbb{P}\bigl(Q_{n,J}\leq t\mid\mathcal{G}_{n}\bigr)-\Phi(t)\Bigr|\longrightarrow 0\qquad\text{in }L^{1}.

The second displayed statement follows immediately, because

\sup_{|t-x|\leq\delta_{n}}\Bigl|\mathbb{P}\bigl(Q_{n,J}\leq t\mid\mathcal{G}_{n}\bigr)-\Phi(t)\Bigr|\leq\sup_{t\in\mathbb{R}}\Bigl|\mathbb{P}\bigl(Q_{n,J}\leq t\mid\mathcal{G}_{n}\bigr)-\Phi(t)\Bigr|.

This completes the proof.
∎

Lemma A.19
.

Fix
d\geq 1
and a coordinate set
J
with
|J|=d
. Let

a_{i}=\sqrt{p}\,Z_{i}\sqrt{B_{i}},\qquad s_{n}^{2}:=\frac{1}{dn}\sum_{i=1}^{n}a_{i}^{2},\qquad u_{p}(y):=\sqrt{2\log p-\log\log p+y}.

Under Assumption
3.1
, if
p\to\infty
,
p=O(n^{\kappa})
for some fixed
\kappa>0
, and
\log p=o(n^{1/5})
, then

s_{n}^{2}=1+O_{p}(n^{-1/2}),\qquad u_{p}(y)^{2}|s_{n}-1|=o_{p}(1),

and

\frac{u_{p}(y)\max_{1\leq i\leq n}|a_{i}|}{\sqrt{\sum_{i=1}^{n}a_{i}^{2}}}=o_{p}(1).

Proof.

Because
B_{i}\sim{\rm Beta}(d/2,(p-d)/2)
and is independent of
Z_{i}
,

\mathbb{E}(a_{i}^{2})=p\,\mathbb{E}(Z_{i}^{2})\mathbb{E}(B_{i})=d.

For fixed
d
, the beta moments satisfy
\sup_{p}\mathbb{E}(pB_{i})^{m}<\infty
for each fixed integer
m\geq 1
. Assumption
3.1
(ii) gives
\sup_{p}\mathbb{E}Z_{i}^{4}<\infty
. Hence

\mathrm{Var}(a_{i}^{2})\leq C_{d}<\infty,

and the ordinary variance bound gives

s_{n}^{2}-1=\frac{1}{dn}\sum_{i=1}^{n}(a_{i}^{2}-d)=O_{p}(n^{-1/2}).

It follows that
s_{n}\to 1
in probability and, because
u_{p}(y)^{2}\asymp\log p
and
\log p=o(n^{1/5})
,

u_{p}(y)^{2}|s_{n}-1|=O_{p}\!\left(\frac{\log p}{\sqrt{n}}\right)=o_{p}(1).

It remains to control the largest coefficient. By Assumption
3.1
(ii) and the beta–gamma representation of
B_{i}
,

\max_{1\leq i\leq n}|Z_{i}|=O_{p}(b_{n,p}),\qquad\max_{1\leq i\leq n}pB_{i}=O_{p}(\log n).

Therefore

\max_{1\leq i\leq n}|a_{i}|=\max_{1\leq i\leq n}\sqrt{p}\,|Z_{i}|\sqrt{B_{i}}=O_{p}\{b_{n,p}\sqrt{\log n}\}.

Since
\sum_{i=1}^{n}a_{i}^{2}=dn\{1+o_{p}(1)\}
and
u_{p}(y)^{2}\asymp\log p
, we obtain

\frac{u_{p}(y)\max_{1\leq i\leq n}|a_{i}|}{\sqrt{\sum_{i=1}^{n}a_{i}^{2}}}=O_{p}\left(\sqrt{\frac{b_{n,p}^{2}\log p\log n}{n}}\right)=o_{p}(1).

This proves the lemma.
∎

Lemma A.20
.

Fix
d\geq 1
,
J=\{j_{1},\dots,j_{d}\}
, and
y\in\mathbb{R}
. Let

E_{n,J}(y):=\bigcap_{\ell=1}^{d}\{|S_{n,j_{\ell}}|>u_{p}(y)\}.

Then, under Assumption
3.1
,
p\to\infty
,
p=O(n^{\kappa})
for some fixed
\kappa>0
, and
\log p=o(n^{1/5})
,

\frac{\mathbb{P}(E_{n,J}(y)\mid\mathcal{G}_{n})}{\{2\bar{\Phi}(u_{p}(y))\}^{d}}\longrightarrow 1\qquad\text{in }L^{1}.

In particular,

\mathbb{P}(E_{n,J}(y))=\{2\bar{\Phi}(u_{p}(y))\}^{d}\{1+o(1)\}.

Proof.

Condition on
\mathcal{G}_{n}
. We have

\bm{S}_{n,J}=\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}\sum_{i=1}^{n}a_{i}\bm{V}_{i},\qquad\mathrm{Cov}(\bm{S}_{n,J}\mid\mathcal{G}_{n})=s_{n}^{2}\mathbf{I}_{d},\qquad s_{n}^{2}:=\frac{1}{dn}\sum_{i=1}^{n}a_{i}^{2}.

By Lemma
A.19
,

s_{n}^{2}=1+O_{p}(n^{-1/2}),\qquad u_{p}(y)^{2}|s_{n}-1|=o_{p}(1),\qquad\frac{u_{p}(y)\max_{i}|a_{i}|}{\sqrt{\sum_{i}a_{i}^{2}}}=o_{p}(1).

Hence, on an event whose probability tends to one, the fixed coefficient array
\{a_{i}:1\leq i\leq n\}
and the independent bounded summands
a_{i}\bm{V}_{i}
satisfy the normalization and maximal-coefficient conditions required in the fixed-dimensional Cramér-type moderate deviation theorem of Shao, Zhang and Zhang
(
35
, Theorem 2.1)
. Since

u_{p}(y)\asymp\sqrt{\log p}=o(n^{1/6}),

we use its fixed-dimensional rectangular-tail consequence. Equivalently, apply the theorem to each of the finitely many orthants and combine the resulting tail probabilities by inclusion–exclusion. This gives, conditionally on
\mathcal{G}_{n}
,

\mathbb{P}(E_{n,J}(y)\mid\mathcal{G}_{n})=\bigl\{2\bar{\Phi}(u_{p}(y)/s_{n})\bigr\}^{d}\{1+o_{p}(1)\}.

It remains only to replace
s_{n}
by one in the normal tail. By Mills’ ratio, uniformly whenever
u_{p}(y)^{2}|s_{n}-1|=o(1)
,

\frac{\bar{\Phi}(u_{p}(y)/s_{n})}{\bar{\Phi}(u_{p}(y))}=\frac{u_{p}(y)}{u_{p}(y)/s_{n}}\exp\!\left[-\frac{1}{2}\{u_{p}(y)^{2}/s_{n}^{2}-u_{p}(y)^{2}\}\right]\{1+o(1)\}=1+o(1).

Therefore

\bar{\Phi}(u_{p}(y)/s_{n})=\bar{\Phi}(u_{p}(y))\{1+o_{p}(1)\}.

The coefficient regularity events used above may be chosen with complement probability
O(p^{-M})
for every fixed
M>0
, by high-moment concentration for the average of
a_{i}^{2}
and the union-tail bounds for
\max_{i}|a_{i}|
. Taking
M>d+1
and using
\{2\bar{\Phi}(u_{p}(y))\}^{d}\asymp p^{-d}
shows that the contribution of the exceptional coefficient event is negligible after division by
\{2\bar{\Phi}(u_{p}(y))\}^{d}
. Thus the conditional ratio convergence holds in
L^{1}
. Taking expectations then gives the unconditional display.
∎

Proposition A.3
.

Under Assumption
3.1
,
p\to\infty
,
p=O(n^{\kappa})
for some fixed
\kappa\in(0,2)
, and
\log p=o(n^{1/5})
. Fix an integer
d\geq 1
, and let
1\leq j_{1}<\cdots<j_{d}\leq p
be distinct indices. Define

u_{p}(y):=\sqrt{2\log p-\log\log p+y},\qquad y\in\mathbb{R}.

Then, for every fixed
x,y\in\mathbb{R}
,

\mathbb{P}\!\left(\frac{H_{n}}{\sqrt{2p}}\leq x,\bigcap_{\ell=1}^{d}\{|S_{n,j_{\ell}}|>u_{p}(y)\}\right)=\Phi(x)\bigl(2\bar{\Phi}(u_{p}(y))\bigr)^{d}\{1+o(1)\}.

Moreover,

\mathbb{P}\!\left(\bigcap_{\ell=1}^{d}\{|S_{n,j_{\ell}}|>u_{p}(y)\}\right)=\bigl(2\bar{\Phi}(u_{p}(y))\bigr)^{d}\{1+o(1)\}.

Proof.

Fix
J=\{j_{1},\dots,j_{d}\}
and write

E_{n,J}(y):=\bigcap_{\ell=1}^{d}\{|S_{n,j_{\ell}}|>u_{p}(y)\},\qquad\Delta_{n,J}:=\frac{H_{n,J}}{\sqrt{2p}},\qquad Q_{n,J}:=\frac{H_{n,J^{c}}}{\sqrt{2p}}.

By Lemma
A.16
,

\frac{H_{n}}{\sqrt{2p}}=Q_{n,J}+\Delta_{n,J},

and, conditional on
\mathcal{G}_{n}
, the variables
Q_{n,J}
and
E_{n,J}(y)
are independent.

Set

\beta_{n,d}(y):=\{2\bar{\Phi}(u_{p}(y))\}^{d}.

By Lemma
A.17
,

\mathbb{P}\bigl(|\Delta_{n,J}|>\delta_{n}\bigr)=o\bigl(\beta_{n,d}(y)\bigr).

Therefore,

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\!\left(\frac{H_{n}}{\sqrt{2p}}\leq x,\ E_{n,J}(y)\right)

\displaystyle\qquad=\mathbb{P}\!\left(Q_{n,J}+\Delta_{n,J}\leq x,\ E_{n,J}(y),\ |\Delta_{n,J}|\leq\delta_{n}\right)+o\bigl(\beta_{n,d}(y)\bigr).

On the event
\{|\Delta_{n,J}|\leq\delta_{n}\}
we have

\{Q_{n,J}\leq x-\delta_{n}\}\subseteq\{Q_{n,J}+\Delta_{n,J}\leq x\}\subseteq\{Q_{n,J}\leq x+\delta_{n}\},

so conditional independence gives the two-sided bound

\displaystyle E\!\left[\mathbb{P}\bigl(Q_{n,J}\leq x-\delta_{n}\mid\mathcal{G}_{n}\bigr)\mathbb{P}\bigl(E_{n,J}(y)\mid\mathcal{G}_{n}\bigr)\right]-o\bigl(\beta_{n,d}(y)\bigr)

\displaystyle\qquad\leq\mathbb{P}\!\left(\frac{H_{n}}{\sqrt{2p}}\leq x,\ E_{n,J}(y)\right)

\displaystyle\qquad\leq E\!\left[\mathbb{P}\bigl(Q_{n,J}\leq x+\delta_{n}\mid\mathcal{G}_{n}\bigr)\mathbb{P}\bigl(E_{n,J}(y)\mid\mathcal{G}_{n}\bigr)\right]+o\bigl(\beta_{n,d}(y)\bigr).

Now Lemma
A.18
yields

A_{n}^{\pm}:=\mathbb{P}\bigl(Q_{n,J}\leq x\pm\delta_{n}\mid\mathcal{G}_{n}\bigr)-\Phi(x)\longrightarrow 0\qquad\text{in }L^{1},

while Lemma
A.20
gives, with

R_{n,J}:=\frac{\mathbb{P}(E_{n,J}(y)\mid\mathcal{G}_{n})}{\beta_{n,d}(y)},

that
R_{n,J}\to 1
in
L^{1}
. Therefore

\displaystyle E\!\left[\mathbb{P}\bigl(Q_{n,J}\leq x\pm\delta_{n}\mid\mathcal{G}_{n}\bigr)\mathbb{P}\bigl(E_{n,J}(y)\mid\mathcal{G}_{n}\bigr)\right]

\displaystyle\qquad=\beta_{n,d}(y)E\{(\Phi(x)+A_{n}^{\pm})R_{n,J}\}

\displaystyle\qquad=\Phi(x)\beta_{n,d}(y)\{1+o(1)\},

because
|A_{n}^{\pm}|\leq 1
and

E|A_{n}^{\pm}|R_{n,J}\leq E|A_{n}^{\pm}|+E|R_{n,J}-1|\to 0
.
The upper and lower bounds therefore match and imply

\mathbb{P}\!\left(\frac{H_{n}}{\sqrt{2p}}\leq x,E_{n,J}(y)\right)=\Phi(x)\beta_{n,d}(y)\{1+o(1)\},

which is the first claim.

The second claim is exactly the unconditional statement from Lemma
A.20
.
∎

Proposition A.4
.

Under Assumption
3.1
. Suppose

p\to\infty,\qquad p=O(n^{\kappa})\quad\text{for some }\kappa\in(0,2),\qquad\log p=o(n^{1/5}).

Then, for every fixed
x,y\in\mathbb{R}
,

\mathbb{P}\!\left(\frac{H_{n}}{\sqrt{2p}}\leq x,\ \|\bm{S}_{n}\|_{\infty}\leq u_{p}(y)\right)\to\Phi(x)\exp\!\bigl(-\pi^{-1/2}e^{-y/2}\bigr),

where

u_{p}(y):=\sqrt{2\log p-\log\log p+y}.

Consequently,

\left(\frac{H_{n}}{\sqrt{2p}},\ \|\bm{S}_{n}\|_{\infty}\right)

are asymptotically independent.

Proof.

Fix
x,y\in\mathbb{R}
. Write

A_{n}(x):=\left\{\frac{H_{n}}{\sqrt{2p}}\leq x\right\},\qquad B_{n,j}(y):=\{|S_{n,j}|>u_{p}(y)\},\qquad L_{n}(y):=\{\|\bm{S}_{n}\|_{\infty}\leq u_{p}(y)\}.

Then
L_{n}(y)=\bigcap_{j=1}^{p}B_{n,j}(y)^{c}
.

For each fixed integer
d\geq 1
, define

H_{n,d}(x,y):=\sum_{1\leq j_{1}<\cdots<j_{d}\leq p}\mathbb{P}\!\left(A_{n}(x)\cap\bigcap_{\ell=1}^{d}B_{n,j_{\ell}}(y)\right),

and set
H_{n,0}(x,y)=\mathbb{P}(A_{n}(x))
.

By Bonferroni’s inequalities, for every fixed
m\geq 1
,

\sum_{d=0}^{2m+1}(-1)^{d}H_{n,d}(x,y)\leq\mathbb{P}(A_{n}(x)\cap L_{n}(y))\leq\sum_{d=0}^{2m}(-1)^{d}H_{n,d}(x,y).

Hence it suffices to show that, for each fixed
d
,

H_{n,d}(x,y)\to\Phi(x)\frac{\lambda(y)^{d}}{d!},\qquad\lambda(y):=\pi^{-1/2}e^{-y/2}.

To this end, fix
d
and distinct coordinates
j_{1},\dots,j_{d}
. By Proposition
A.3
,

\mathbb{P}\!\left(A_{n}(x)\cap\bigcap_{\ell=1}^{d}B_{n,j_{\ell}}(y)\right)=\Phi(x)\bigl(2\bar{\Phi}(u_{p}(y))\bigr)^{d}\{1+o(1)\}.

Since the coordinates are exchangeable under Assumption
3.1
, the left-hand side does not depend on the specific choice of
\{j_{1},\dots,j_{d}\}
. Therefore

H_{n,d}(x,y)=\binom{p}{d}\Phi(x)\bigl(2\bar{\Phi}(u_{p}(y))\bigr)^{d}\{1+o(1)\}.

By Mills’ ratio,

2p\,\bar{\Phi}(u_{p}(y))\to\lambda(y)=\pi^{-1/2}e^{-y/2},

hence

\binom{p}{d}\bigl(2\bar{\Phi}(u_{p}(y))\bigr)^{d}\to\frac{\lambda(y)^{d}}{d!}.

Thus

H_{n,d}(x,y)\to\Phi(x)\frac{\lambda(y)^{d}}{d!}.

Now let
n\to\infty
in the Bonferroni bounds, and then let
m\to\infty
. Since

\sum_{d=0}^{\infty}(-1)^{d}\frac{\lambda(y)^{d}}{d!}=e^{-\lambda(y)},

we conclude that

\mathbb{P}\!\left(\frac{H_{n}}{\sqrt{2p}}\leq x,\ \|\bm{S}_{n}\|_{\infty}\leq u_{p}(y)\right)\to\Phi(x)e^{-\lambda(y)}=\Phi(x)\exp\!\bigl(-\pi^{-1/2}e^{-y/2}\bigr),

which proves the desired asymptotic independence.
∎

A.5.2
Proof of Theorem
3.4

Proof of Theorem
3.4
.

By Lemma
A.14
,

\frac{T_{\mathrm{sum}}-p}{\sqrt{2p}}=\frac{H_{n}}{\sqrt{2p}}+o_{p}(1).

By Lemma
A.11
,

T_{\max}=\|\bm{S}_{n}\|_{\infty}^{2}-2\log p+\log\log p+o_{p}(1).

Proposition
A.4
therefore yields

\left(\frac{T_{\mathrm{sum}}-p}{\sqrt{2p}},\ T_{\max}\right)\xrightarrow{d}(Z_{1},Z_{2}),

where
Z_{1}\sim N(0,1)
,
Z_{2}
has cdf
F(x)=\exp(-\pi^{-1/2}e^{-x/2})
, and
Z_{1}
is independent of
Z_{2}
.

For the p-values, the continuous mapping theorem gives

(P_{\rm sum},P_{\max})\xrightarrow{d}(U_{1},U_{2}),

where
U_{1},U_{2}
are independent
U(0,1)
random variables. Consequently,

\tan\{\pi(1/2-U_{1})\}\quad\text{and}\quad\tan\{\pi(1/2-U_{2})\}

are independent standard Cauchy random variables. By the stability of the Cauchy distribution, their equally weighted average is again standard Cauchy. Thus

T_{\rm cau}=\frac{1}{2}\tan\!\bigl(\pi(1/2-P_{\rm sum})\bigr)+\frac{1}{2}\tan\!\bigl(\pi(1/2-P_{\max})\bigr)

converges in distribution to a standard Cauchy random variable; see also Liu and Xie
22
. Hence

P_{\rm cau}:=\frac{1}{2}-\frac{1}{\pi}\arctan(T_{\rm cau})\xrightarrow{d}U(0,1).

This completes the proof.
∎

A.6
Proof of Theorem
3.5

Proof of Theorem
3.5
.

Set

\bm{\delta}_{n}=\hat{\bm{g}}_{n}-\bm{g}_{n}^{\rm or},\qquad\bm{u}_{n}=\tilde{\kappa}_{p}\bar{\bm{U}}+\kappa_{p}n^{-1/2}\bm{C}_{n}.

Proposition
3.1
gives

\bm{\delta}_{n}=\bm{u}_{n}+\bm{b}_{n}^{\rm hard}+\bm{a}_{n}.

(36)

Since
\zeta_{1}\asymp p^{-1/2}
,

|\kappa_{p}|=|\tilde{\kappa}_{p}|\zeta_{1}\leq C|\tilde{\kappa}_{p}|p^{-1/2}.

(37)

The oracle bounds used below are

\displaystyle\|\bm{g}_{n}^{\rm or}\|_{1}

\displaystyle=O_{p}(pn^{-1/2}),

\displaystyle\frac{n}{\sqrt{p}}|(\bm{g}_{n}^{\rm or})^{\top}\bar{\bm{U}}|

\displaystyle=O_{p}\left(n^{-1/2}+p^{-1/2}+\frac{\log p}{\sqrt{n}}\right),

(38)

\displaystyle\frac{n}{\sqrt{p}}\|\bar{\bm{U}}\|_{2}^{2}

\displaystyle=O_{p}(p^{-1/2}),

\displaystyle\|\bm{C}_{n}\|_{\infty}

\displaystyle=O_{p}(\mathfrak{c}_{n}).

Equations (
37
)–(
38
) imply

\displaystyle\frac{n}{\sqrt{p}}|(\bm{g}_{n}^{\rm or})^{\top}\bm{u}_{n}|

\displaystyle\leq|\tilde{\kappa}_{p}|\frac{n}{\sqrt{p}}|(\bm{g}_{n}^{\rm or})^{\top}\bar{\bm{U}}|+\frac{n}{\sqrt{p}}|\kappa_{p}|n^{-1/2}\|\bm{g}_{n}^{\rm or}\|_{1}\|\bm{C}_{n}\|_{\infty}

(39)

\displaystyle=O_{p}\left[|\tilde{\kappa}_{p}|\left(n^{-1/2}+p^{-1/2}+\frac{\log p}{\sqrt{n}}\right)+|\tilde{\kappa}_{p}|\mathfrak{c}_{n}\right],

\displaystyle\frac{n}{\sqrt{p}}\|\bm{u}_{n}\|_{2}^{2}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{2n}{\sqrt{p}}|\tilde{\kappa}_{p}|^{2}\|\bar{\bm{U}}\|_{2}^{2}+\frac{2n}{\sqrt{p}}|\kappa_{p}|^{2}n^{-1}\|\bm{C}_{n}\|_{2}^{2}

\displaystyle\leq O_{p}\left(\frac{|\tilde{\kappa}_{p}|^{2}}{\sqrt{p}}\right)+\frac{C|\tilde{\kappa}_{p}|^{2}}{p\sqrt{p}}\|\bm{C}_{n}\|_{2}^{2}

\displaystyle\leq O_{p}\left(\frac{|\tilde{\kappa}_{p}|^{2}}{\sqrt{p}}\right)+O_{p}\left(\frac{|\tilde{\kappa}_{p}|^{2}\mathfrak{c}_{n}^{2}}{\sqrt{p}}\right).

By Proposition
3.1
,

\displaystyle\frac{n}{\sqrt{p}}\left\{|(\bm{g}_{n}^{\rm or})^{\top}\bm{b}_{n}^{\rm hard}|+\|\bm{b}_{n}^{\rm hard}\|_{2}^{2}\right\}

\displaystyle=O_{p}\left(\tau_{n}+\frac{\sqrt{p}}{n}+\frac{\tau_{n}^{2}}{\sqrt{p}}\right),

\displaystyle\frac{n}{\sqrt{p}}\left\{|(\bm{g}_{n}^{\rm or})^{\top}\bm{a}_{n}|+|\bm{d}_{n}^{\top}\bm{a}_{n}|+\|\bm{a}_{n}\|_{2}^{2}\right\}

\displaystyle=O_{p}(\mathfrak{A}_{S,n}).

The cross products satisfy

\displaystyle\frac{n}{\sqrt{p}}|\bm{u}_{n}^{\top}\bm{b}_{n}^{\rm hard}|

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{2}\frac{n}{\sqrt{p}}\|\bm{u}_{n}\|_{2}^{2}+\frac{1}{2}\frac{n}{\sqrt{p}}\|\bm{b}_{n}^{\rm hard}\|_{2}^{2},

(40)

\displaystyle\frac{n}{\sqrt{p}}|\bm{u}_{n}^{\top}\bm{a}_{n}|

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{2}\frac{n}{\sqrt{p}}\|\bm{u}_{n}\|_{2}^{2}+\frac{1}{2}\frac{n}{\sqrt{p}}\|\bm{a}_{n}\|_{2}^{2}.

Combining (
39
)–(
40
),

\frac{n}{\sqrt{p}}\left\{|(\bm{g}_{n}^{\rm or})^{\top}\bm{\delta}_{n}|+\|\bm{\delta}_{n}\|_{2}^{2}\right\}=O_{p}(\mathfrak{A}_{S,n}).

The identity

\displaystyle\hat{T}_{\rm sum}-T_{\rm sum}

\displaystyle=n\|\bm{g}_{n}^{\rm or}+\bm{\delta}_{n}\|_{2}^{2}-n\|\bm{g}_{n}^{\rm or}\|_{2}^{2}

\displaystyle=2n(\bm{g}_{n}^{\rm or})^{\top}\bm{\delta}_{n}+n\|\bm{\delta}_{n}\|_{2}^{2}

gives

\left|\frac{\hat{T}_{\rm sum}-T_{\rm sum}}{\sqrt{2p}}\right|\leq\sqrt{2}\frac{n}{\sqrt{p}}|(\bm{g}_{n}^{\rm or})^{\top}\bm{\delta}_{n}|+\frac{1}{\sqrt{2}}\frac{n}{\sqrt{p}}\|\bm{\delta}_{n}\|_{2}^{2}=O_{p}(\mathfrak{A}_{S,n}).

(41)

Because
\mathfrak{A}_{S,n}\to 0
and Theorem
3.2
gives
(T_{\rm sum}-p)/\sqrt{2p}\xrightarrow{d}N(0,1)
,

\frac{\hat{T}_{\rm sum}-p}{\sqrt{2p}}=\frac{T_{\rm sum}-p}{\sqrt{2p}}+O_{p}(\mathfrak{A}_{S,n})\xrightarrow{d}N(0,1).

∎

A.7
Proof of Theorem
3.6

Proof of Theorem
3.6
.

Use the same
\bm{\delta}_{n}
and
\bm{u}_{n}
as in (
36
). From Assumption
3.1
, Proposition
3.1
and (
37
),

\displaystyle\sqrt{n}\|\bm{\delta}_{n}\|_{\infty}

\displaystyle\leq|\tilde{\kappa}_{p}|\sqrt{n}\|\bar{\bm{U}}\|_{\infty}+|\kappa_{p}|\|\bm{C}_{n}\|_{\infty}+\sqrt{n}\|\bm{b}_{n}^{\rm hard}\|_{\infty}+\sqrt{n}\|\bm{a}_{n}\|_{\infty}

\displaystyle=O_{p}\left(|\tilde{\kappa}_{p}|\sqrt{\frac{\log p}{p}}+\frac{|\tilde{\kappa}_{p}|\mathfrak{c}_{n}}{\sqrt{p}}+\tau_{n}\sqrt{\log p}+\sqrt{\frac{\log p}{n}}+\frac{\mathfrak{A}_{M,n}}{\sqrt{\log p}}\right)

\displaystyle=O_{p}\left(\frac{\mathfrak{A}_{M,n}}{\sqrt{\log p}}\right).

The oracle maximum satisfies

\sqrt{n}\|\bm{g}_{n}^{\rm or}\|_{\infty}=O_{p}(\sqrt{\log p}).

For any two vectors
\bm{x},\bm{y}\in\mathbb{R}^{p}
,

\left|\|\bm{x}\|_{\infty}^{2}-\|\bm{y}\|_{\infty}^{2}\right|\leq 2\|\bm{y}\|_{\infty}\|\bm{x}-\bm{y}\|_{\infty}+\|\bm{x}-\bm{y}\|_{\infty}^{2}.

With
\bm{x}=\sqrt{n}\hat{\bm{g}}_{n}
and
\bm{y}=\sqrt{n}\bm{g}_{n}^{\rm or}
,

\displaystyle|\hat{T}_{\max}-T_{\max}|

\displaystyle\leq 2\sqrt{n}\|\bm{g}_{n}^{\rm or}\|_{\infty}\sqrt{n}\|\bm{\delta}_{n}\|_{\infty}+n\|\bm{\delta}_{n}\|_{\infty}^{2}

(42)

\displaystyle=O_{p}(\sqrt{\log p})O_{p}\left(\frac{\mathfrak{A}_{M,n}}{\sqrt{\log p}}\right)+O_{p}\left(\frac{\mathfrak{A}_{M,n}^{2}}{\log p}\right)

\displaystyle=O_{p}(\mathfrak{A}_{M,n}).

Since
\mathfrak{A}_{M,n}\to 0
, (
42
) and Theorem
3.3
imply, for every
x\in\mathbb{R}
,

\mathbb{P}(\hat{T}_{\max}\leq x)=\mathbb{P}(T_{\max}+O_{p}(\mathfrak{A}_{M,n})\leq x)\to\exp\{-\pi^{-1/2}e^{-x/2}\}.

∎

A.8
Proof of Corollary
3.1

Proof of Corollary
3.1
.

Equations (
41
) and (
42
) give

\frac{\hat{T}_{\rm sum}-p}{\sqrt{2p}}=\frac{T_{\rm sum}-p}{\sqrt{2p}}+O_{p}(\mathfrak{A}_{S,n}),\qquad\hat{T}_{\max}=T_{\max}+O_{p}(\mathfrak{A}_{M,n}).

Together with
\mathfrak{A}_{S,n}\to 0
,
\mathfrak{A}_{M,n}\to 0
and Theorem
3.4
,

\left(\frac{\hat{T}_{\rm sum}-p}{\sqrt{2p}},\hat{T}_{\max}\right)\xrightarrow{d}(Z,G),\qquad Z\mathrel{\perp\mspace{-10mu}\perp}G.

The p-value transformations satisfy

\hat{P}_{\rm sum}=1-\Phi\left(\frac{\hat{T}_{\rm sum}-p}{\sqrt{2p}}\right),\qquad\hat{P}_{\max}=1-F_{G}(\hat{T}_{\max}),

and therefore

(\hat{P}_{\rm sum},\hat{P}_{\max})\xrightarrow{d}(U_{1},U_{2}),\qquad U_{1}\mathrel{\perp\mspace{-10mu}\perp}U_{2},\qquad U_{1},U_{2}\sim U(0,1).

Hence

\frac{1}{2}\tan\{\pi(1/2-\hat{P}_{\rm sum})\}+\frac{1}{2}\tan\{\pi(1/2-\hat{P}_{\max})\}\xrightarrow{d}C_{0},\qquad C_{0}\sim\text{standard Cauchy},

and

\hat{P}_{\rm cau}=\frac{1}{2}-\frac{1}{\pi}\arctan\left[\frac{1}{2}\tan\{\pi(1/2-\hat{P}_{\rm sum})\}+\frac{1}{2}\tan\{\pi(1/2-\hat{P}_{\max})\}\right]\xrightarrow{d}U(0,1).

∎

A.9
Proof of Proposition
3.2

Proof.

From (
18
)–(
19
),

\mathbf{\Sigma}^{-1/2}(\bm{X}_{i}-\bm{\mu})=R_{1i}\bm{U}_{0i},\qquad R_{1i}>0,\qquad\mathcal{U}\{\mathbf{\Sigma}^{-1/2}(\bm{X}_{i}-\bm{\mu})\}=\bm{U}_{0i}.

Since
\bm{U}_{0i}\sim\mathrm{Unif}(\mathbb{S}^{p-1})
,

\mathbb{E}\bm{U}_{0i}=\bm{0},\qquad\mathbb{E}(\bm{U}_{0i}\bm{U}_{0i}^{\top})=p^{-1}\mathbf{I}_{p},\qquad p\mathbb{E}(\bm{U}_{0i}\bm{U}_{0i}^{\top})=\mathbf{I}_{p}.

Thus

\mathbb{E}\mathcal{U}\{\mathbf{\Sigma}^{-1/2}(\bm{X}_{i}-\bm{\mu})\}=\bm{0},\qquad p\mathbb{E}\left[\mathcal{U}\{\mathbf{\Sigma}^{-1/2}(\bm{X}_{i}-\bm{\mu})\}\mathcal{U}\{\mathbf{\Sigma}^{-1/2}(\bm{X}_{i}-\bm{\mu})\}^{\top}\right]=\mathbf{I}_{p}.

For any unit vector
\bm{x}
, because
R_{1i}=R_{0i}\exp\{\delta_{n}s_{A}(\bm{U}_{0i})\}
and
R_{0i}\mathrel{\perp\mspace{-10mu}\perp}\bm{U}_{0i}
,

\bm{x}^{\top}\mathbf{A}_{1,n}\bm{x}=\mathbb{E}(R_{0i}^{-1})\mathbb{E}\left[e^{-\delta_{n}s_{A}(\bm{U}_{0i})}\{1-(\bm{x}^{\top}\bm{U}_{0i})^{2}\}\right].

The spherical moment generating bound

\mathbb{E}\exp\{ts_{A}(\bm{U}_{0i})\}\leq\exp(Ct^{2}/p),\qquad t\in\mathbb{R},

and
\delta_{n}^{2}/p\leq C_{\delta}
imply

c\leq\mathbb{E}e^{-\delta_{n}s_{A}(\bm{U}_{0i})}\leq C,\qquad\mathbb{E}e^{-2\delta_{n}s_{A}(\bm{U}_{0i})}\leq C.

Also,

\mathbb{E}(\bm{x}^{\top}\bm{U}_{0i})^{2}=p^{-1},\qquad\mathbb{E}(\bm{x}^{\top}\bm{U}_{0i})^{4}=\frac{3}{p(p+2)}.

Hence

\mathbb{E}\left[e^{-\delta_{n}s_{A}(\bm{U}_{0i})}(\bm{x}^{\top}\bm{U}_{0i})^{2}\right]\leq\{\mathbb{E}e^{-2\delta_{n}s_{A}(\bm{U}_{0i})}\}^{1/2}\{\mathbb{E}(\bm{x}^{\top}\bm{U}_{0i})^{4}\}^{1/2}\leq Cp^{-1},

and

cp^{-1/2}\leq\lambda_{\min}(\mathbf{A}_{1,n})\leq\lambda_{\max}(\mathbf{A}_{1,n})\leq Cp^{-1/2},\qquad\|\mathbf{A}_{1,n}^{-1}\|_{op}\leq C\sqrt{p}.

At the true HR target,

\mathbb{E}\{\bm{U}_{0i}\bm{U}_{0i}^{\top}\}=p^{-1}\mathbf{I}_{p},\qquad\mathcal{U}(\bm{X}_{i}-\bm{\mu})=\mathcal{U}(\mathbf{\Sigma}^{1/2}\bm{U}_{0i}).

Let

\mathbf{S}_{0,1}=\mathbb{E}\left[\mathcal{U}(\mathbf{\Sigma}^{1/2}\bm{U}_{0i})\mathcal{U}(\mathbf{\Sigma}^{1/2}\bm{U}_{0i})^{\top}\right].

Since the radius cancels in every spatial sign,

\mathbf{S}_{0,1,H_{1}}=\mathbf{S}_{0,H_{0}},\qquad\|\hat{\mathbf{S}}_{0}-\mathbf{S}_{0,1}\|_{\max}=O_{p}\left(\sqrt{\frac{\log p}{n}}+p^{-1/2}\right).

The graphical-lasso objective in Algorithm
1
and Assumption
3.2
(iii)–(v) therefore give

\|\hat{\mathbf{\Omega}}-\mathbf{\Omega}\|_{L_{1}}\leq Cs_{0}(p)\lambda_{n}^{1-q}=O_{p}(\tau_{n}),\qquad\|\hat{\mathbf{\Omega}}-\mathbf{\Omega}\|_{op}=O_{p}(\tau_{n}).

Thus

\mathbf{B}_{1}=\hat{\mathbf{\Sigma}}^{-1/2}\mathbf{\Sigma}^{1/2}-\mathbf{I}_{p},\qquad\|\mathbf{B}_{1}\|_{op}=O_{p}(\tau_{n}).

Let

\hat{\bm{Y}}_{i}=\hat{\mathbf{\Sigma}}^{-1/2}(\bm{X}_{i}-\hat{\bm{\mu}})=(\mathbf{I}_{p}+\mathbf{B}_{1})R_{1i}\bm{U}_{0i}-\bm{v}_{1}=R_{1i}\bm{U}_{0i}+\bm{h}_{i},

where

\bm{h}_{i}=R_{1i}\mathbf{B}_{1}\bm{U}_{0i}-\bm{v}_{1}.

For
R>0
,
\bm{u}\in\mathbb{S}^{p-1}
and
\|\bm{h}\|_{2}\leq R/2
,

\mathcal{U}(R\bm{u}+\bm{h})=\bm{u}+R^{-1}(\mathbf{I}_{p}-\bm{u}\bm{u}^{\top})\bm{h}+\bm{q}(R,\bm{u},\bm{h}),

\|\bm{q}(R,\bm{u},\bm{h})\|_{\infty}\leq CR^{-2}\|\bm{h}\|_{2}^{2}\left(\|\bm{u}\|_{\infty}+R^{-1}\|\bm{h}\|_{\infty}\right).

The HR location equation gives

\bm{0}=\bar{\bm{U}}_{0}+\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}(\mathbf{I}_{p}-\bm{U}_{0i}\bm{U}_{0i}^{\top})\mathbf{B}_{1}\bm{U}_{0i}-\mathbf{Q}_{1,n}\bm{v}_{1}+\bar{\bm{q}}_{n},

where

\mathbf{Q}_{1,n}=\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}R_{1i}^{-1}(\mathbf{I}_{p}-\bm{U}_{0i}\bm{U}_{0i}^{\top}),\qquad\bar{\bm{q}}_{n}=\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\bm{q}(R_{1i},\bm{U}_{0i},\bm{h}_{i}).

The assumed moment bounds and spherical concentration inequalities imply

\|\mathbf{Q}_{1,n}-\mathbf{A}_{1,n}\|_{\max}=O_{p}\left(p^{-1/2}\sqrt{\frac{\log p}{n}}+p^{-3/2}\sqrt{\frac{\log p}{n}}\right),

\|\bar{\bm{U}}_{0}\|_{\infty}=O_{p}\left(\sqrt{\frac{\log p}{np}}\right),

\left\|\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}(\mathbf{I}_{p}-\bm{U}_{0i}\bm{U}_{0i}^{\top})\mathbf{B}_{1}\bm{U}_{0i}\right\|_{\infty}=O_{p}\left(\tau_{n}\sqrt{\frac{\log p}{np}}\right).

Since

\mathbf{Q}_{1,n}^{-1}=\mathbf{A}_{1,n}^{-1}+\mathbf{A}_{1,n}^{-1}(\mathbf{A}_{1,n}-\mathbf{Q}_{1,n})\mathbf{A}_{1,n}^{-1}+\mathbf{R}_{Q,n},

\|\mathbf{R}_{Q,n}\|_{op}\leq C\|\mathbf{A}_{1,n}^{-1}\|_{op}^{3}\|\mathbf{Q}_{1,n}-\mathbf{A}_{1,n}\|_{op}^{2},

we obtain

\bm{v}_{1}=\mathbf{A}_{1,n}^{-1}\bar{\bm{U}}_{0}+n^{-1/2}\bm{C}_{1,n},\qquad\|\bm{C}_{1,n}\|_{\infty}=O_{p}(\mathfrak{c}_{n}).

Define

\bm{\phi}_{1}(\bm{y})=(\log\|\bm{y}\|_{2}-m_{1,n})\frac{\bm{y}}{\|\bm{y}\|_{2}},\qquad\bm{\phi}_{1i}=(L_{1i}-m_{1,n})\bm{U}_{0i}.

For
\bm{y}=R\bm{u}
,

D\bm{\phi}_{1}(R\bm{u})[\bm{h}]=\left(\frac{\bm{u}^{\top}\bm{h}}{R}\right)\bm{u}+(L-m_{1,n})R^{-1}(\mathbf{I}_{p}-\bm{u}\bm{u}^{\top})\bm{h}.

Substituting
\bm{h}_{i}=R_{1i}\mathbf{B}_{1}\bm{U}_{0i}-\bm{v}_{1}
yields

D\bm{\phi}_{1}(R_{1i}\bm{U}_{0i})[\bm{h}_{i}]=(L_{1i}-m_{1,n})(\mathbf{I}_{p}-\bm{U}_{0i}\bm{U}_{0i}^{\top})\mathbf{B}_{1}\bm{U}_{0i}+\bm{U}_{0i}(\bm{U}_{0i}^{\top}\mathbf{B}_{1}\bm{U}_{0i})-\mathbf{N}_{1i}\bm{v}_{1},

where

\mathbf{N}_{1i}=R_{1i}^{-1}\left\{(L_{1i}-m_{1,n})\mathbf{I}_{p}+[1-(L_{1i}-m_{1,n})]\bm{U}_{0i}\bm{U}_{0i}^{\top}\right\}.

The Taylor remainder satisfies

\displaystyle\left\|\bm{\phi}_{1}(R_{1i}\bm{U}_{0i}+\bm{h}_{i})-\bm{\phi}_{1i}-D\bm{\phi}_{1}(R_{1i}\bm{U}_{0i})[\bm{h}_{i}]\right\|_{\infty}

\displaystyle\leq C(1+|L_{1i}-m_{1,n}|)R_{1i}^{-2}\|\bm{h}_{i}\|_{2}^{2}\{\|\bm{U}_{0i}\|_{\infty}+R_{1i}^{-1}\|\bm{h}_{i}\|_{\infty}\}.

Consequently,

\hat{\bm{g}}_{n}^{(1)}-\bm{g}_{n}^{(1),\rm or}=-(\sigma_{1,n}\sigma_{U})^{-1}\mathbf{M}_{1,n}\mathbf{A}_{1,n}^{-1}\bar{\bm{U}}_{0}+\bm{\Delta}_{L,n}+\bm{\Delta}_{\Sigma,n}+\bm{\Delta}_{R,n},

where

\bm{\Delta}_{L,n}=-(\sigma_{1,n}\sigma_{U})^{-1}\mathbf{M}_{1,n}n^{-1/2}\bm{C}_{1,n}-(\sigma_{1,n}\sigma_{U})^{-1}\left(\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\mathbf{N}_{1i}-\mathbf{M}_{1,n}\right)\bm{v}_{1},

\bm{\Delta}_{\Sigma,n}=(\sigma_{1,n}\sigma_{U})^{-1}\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\left[(L_{1i}-m_{1,n})(\mathbf{I}_{p}-\bm{U}_{0i}\bm{U}_{0i}^{\top})\mathbf{B}_{1}\bm{U}_{0i}+\bm{U}_{0i}(\bm{U}_{0i}^{\top}\mathbf{B}_{1}\bm{U}_{0i})\right],

and
\bm{\Delta}_{R,n}
contains the Taylor, centering and empirical-standardization remainders. The leading term is
\mathbf{H}_{1,n}\bar{\bm{U}}_{0}
, and therefore

\|\mathbf{H}_{1,n}\bar{\bm{U}}_{0}\|_{2}\leq\|\mathbf{H}_{1,n}\|_{op}\|\bar{\bm{U}}_{0}\|_{2}=O_{p}(\mathfrak{H}_{2,n}n^{-1/2}),

\|\mathbf{H}_{1,n}\bar{\bm{U}}_{0}\|_{\infty}\leq\|\mathbf{H}_{1,n}\|_{L_{1}}\|\bar{\bm{U}}_{0}\|_{\infty}=O_{p}\left(\mathfrak{H}_{\infty,n}\sqrt{\frac{\log p}{np}}\right).

The
\bm{C}_{1,n}
term gives

\|(\sigma_{1,n}\sigma_{U})^{-1}\mathbf{M}_{1,n}n^{-1/2}\bm{C}_{1,n}\|_{2}\leq n^{-1/2}\mathfrak{h}_{2,n}\sqrt{p}\,\|\bm{C}_{1,n}\|_{\infty}=O_{p}\left(\frac{\sqrt{p}\,\mathfrak{h}_{2,n}\mathfrak{c}_{n}}{\sqrt{n}}\right),

\|(\sigma_{1,n}\sigma_{U})^{-1}\mathbf{M}_{1,n}n^{-1/2}\bm{C}_{1,n}\|_{\infty}\leq n^{-1/2}\mathfrak{h}_{\infty,n}\|\bm{C}_{1,n}\|_{\infty}=O_{p}\left(\frac{\mathfrak{h}_{\infty,n}\mathfrak{c}_{n}}{\sqrt{n}}\right).

The empirical fluctuation of
n^{-1}\sum_{i}\mathbf{N}_{1i}
gives

\left\|(\sigma_{1,n}\sigma_{U})^{-1}\left(\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\mathbf{N}_{1i}-\mathbf{M}_{1,n}\right)\bm{v}_{1}\right\|_{2}=O_{p}\left((1+\rho_{1,n})\frac{\tau_{n}}{\sqrt{n}}+(1+\rho_{1,n})^{2}\frac{p}{n}\right),

\left\|(\sigma_{1,n}\sigma_{U})^{-1}\left(\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\mathbf{N}_{1i}-\mathbf{M}_{1,n}\right)\bm{v}_{1}\right\|_{\infty}=O_{p}\left((1+\rho_{1,n})\tau_{n}\sqrt{\frac{\log p}{n}}+(1+\rho_{1,n})^{2}\frac{\log p}{n}\right).

The non-null mean of the shape derivative is

\mathbb{E}\left[(L_{1i}-m_{1,n})(\mathbf{I}_{p}-\bm{U}_{0i}\bm{U}_{0i}^{\top})\mathbf{B}_{1}\bm{U}_{0i}\mid\mathbf{B}_{1}\right]=\delta_{n}\mathbb{E}\left[s_{A}(\bm{U}_{0i})(\mathbf{I}_{p}-\bm{U}_{0i}\bm{U}_{0i}^{\top})\mathbf{B}_{1}\bm{U}_{0i}\mid\mathbf{B}_{1}\right],

\left\|\mathbb{E}\left[s_{A}(\bm{U}_{0i})(\mathbf{I}_{p}-\bm{U}_{0i}\bm{U}_{0i}^{\top})\mathbf{B}_{1}\bm{U}_{0i}\mid\mathbf{B}_{1}\right]\right\|_{2}\leq Cp^{-1}\|\mathbf{B}_{1}\|_{op},

and

\mathbb{E}\{\bm{U}_{0i}(\bm{U}_{0i}^{\top}\mathbf{B}_{1}\bm{U}_{0i})\mid\mathbf{B}_{1}\}=\bm{0}.

Thus

\|\bm{\Delta}_{\Sigma,n}\|_{2}=O_{p}\left((1+\rho_{1,n})\left\{\frac{\delta_{n}\tau_{n}}{\sqrt{p}}+\frac{\tau_{n}}{\sqrt{n}}\right\}\right),

\|\bm{\Delta}_{\Sigma,n}\|_{\infty}=O_{p}\left((1+\rho_{1,n})\frac{\delta_{n}\tau_{n}}{\sqrt{p}}+(1+\rho_{1,n})\tau_{n}\sqrt{\frac{\log p}{n}}\right).

The Taylor, centering and empirical-standardization terms obey

\|\bm{\Delta}_{R,n}\|_{2}=O_{p}\left((1+\rho_{1,n})^{2}\left\{\tau_{n}^{2}+\frac{p}{n}\right\}\right),\qquad\|\bm{\Delta}_{R,n}\|_{\infty}=O_{p}\left((1+\rho_{1,n})^{2}\left\{\tau_{n}^{2}+\frac{\log p}{n}\right\}\right).

Combining the preceding displays gives

\|\hat{\bm{g}}_{n}^{(1)}-\bm{g}_{n}^{(1),\rm or}\|_{2}=O_{p}(\mathfrak{R}_{2,n}^{(1)}),\qquad\|\hat{\bm{g}}_{n}^{(1)}-\bm{g}_{n}^{(1),\rm or}\|_{\infty}=O_{p}(\mathfrak{R}_{\infty,n}^{(1)}).

∎

A.10
Proof of Theorem
3.7

Proof.

Let

S_{A}(\bm{u})=s_{n}^{-1/2}\sum_{k\in A}u_{k},\qquad L_{1i}=L_{0i}+\delta_{n}S_{A}(\bm{U}_{0i}),\qquad m_{0,n}=\mathbb{E}L_{0i}.

For
\bm{U}_{0i}\sim\mathrm{Unif}(\mathbb{S}^{p-1})
,

\mathbb{E}U_{0ij}=0,\qquad\mathbb{E}(U_{0ij}U_{0ik})=p^{-1}\mathbf{1}(j=k),\qquad\mathbb{E}S_{A}(\bm{U}_{0i})=0,

\mathrm{Var}\{S_{A}(\bm{U}_{0i})\}=s_{n}^{-1}\sum_{j,k\in A}\mathbb{E}(U_{0ij}U_{0ik})=p^{-1},

\mathrm{Cov}\{S_{A}(\bm{U}_{0i}),U_{0ij}\}=s_{n}^{-1/2}\sum_{k\in A}\mathbb{E}(U_{0ik}U_{0ij})=s_{n}^{-1/2}p^{-1}\mathbf{1}(j\in A).

Since
L_{0i}\mathrel{\perp\mspace{-10mu}\perp}\bm{U}_{0i}
,

\mathbb{E}L_{1i}=m_{0,n},\qquad\sigma_{1,n}^{2}=\mathrm{Var}(L_{0i})+\delta_{n}^{2}\mathrm{Var}\{S_{A}(\bm{U}_{0i})\}=\sigma_{0,n}^{2}+\delta_{n}^{2}p^{-1},

\mathrm{Cov}(L_{1i},U_{0ij})=\delta_{n}\mathrm{Cov}\{S_{A}(\bm{U}_{0i}),U_{0ij}\}=\delta_{n}s_{n}^{-1/2}p^{-1}\mathbf{1}(j\in A).

Because
\sigma_{U}^{2}=p^{-1}
,

\gamma_{j}=\frac{\mathrm{Cov}(L_{1i},U_{0ij})}{\sigma_{1,n}\sigma_{U}}=\frac{\delta_{n}\mathbf{1}(j\in A)}{\sqrt{s_{n}p}\,\sigma_{1,n}},

\|\bm{\gamma}\|_{2}^{2}=s_{n}\frac{\delta_{n}^{2}}{s_{n}p\sigma_{1,n}^{2}}=\frac{\delta_{n}^{2}}{p\sigma_{0,n}^{2}+\delta_{n}^{2}},\qquad\|\bm{\gamma}\|_{\infty}^{2}=\frac{\delta_{n}^{2}}{s_{n}(p\sigma_{0,n}^{2}+\delta_{n}^{2})}.

Put

\bm{V}_{i}=\frac{(L_{1i}-m_{0,n})\bm{U}_{0i}}{\sigma_{1,n}\sigma_{U}},\qquad\bar{\bm{V}}=n^{-1}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\bm{V}_{i},\qquad\bm{Z}_{n}=\sqrt{n}(\bar{\bm{V}}-\bm{\gamma}).

The centered empirical correlation satisfies

\hat{\gamma}_{j}^{\rm or}=\frac{\bar{V}_{j}-B_{L,n}B_{U,n,j}}{\{(1+A_{L,n}-B_{L,n}^{2})(1+A_{U,n,j}-B_{U,n,j}^{2})\}^{1/2}},

where

A_{L,n}=n^{-1}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\frac{(L_{1i}-m_{0,n})^{2}}{\sigma_{1,n}^{2}}-1,\qquad B_{L,n}=\frac{\bar{L}_{1}-m_{0,n}}{\sigma_{1,n}},

A_{U,n,j}=pn^{-1}\sum_{i=1}^{n}U_{0ij}^{2}-1,\qquad B_{U,n,j}=\sqrt{p}\,\bar{U}_{0j}.

The moment and truncation conditions for the standardized log-radius, together with the spherical concentration inequalities, give

A_{L,n}=O_{p}(n^{-1/2}),\qquad B_{L,n}=O_{p}(n^{-1/2}),

\max_{1\leq j\leq p}|A_{U,n,j}|=O_{p}\left(\sqrt{\frac{\log p}{n}}+\frac{\log p}{n}\right),\qquad\max_{1\leq j\leq p}|B_{U,n,j}|=O_{p}\left(\sqrt{\frac{\log p}{n}}\right),

\max_{1\leq j\leq p}|\bar{V}_{j}-\gamma_{j}|=O_{p}\left(\sqrt{\frac{\log p}{n}}\right).

Consequently,

\|\hat{\bm{g}}_{n}^{(1),\rm or}-\bm{\gamma}-n^{-1/2}\bm{Z}_{n}\|_{\infty}=O_{p}\left(\frac{\log p}{n}+\|\bm{\gamma}\|_{\infty}\sqrt{\frac{\log p}{n}}\right),

\|\hat{\bm{g}}_{n}^{(1),\rm or}-\bm{\gamma}-n^{-1/2}\bm{Z}_{n}\|_{2}=O_{p}\left(\frac{\sqrt{p}\log p}{n}+\|\bm{\gamma}\|_{2}\sqrt{\frac{\log p}{n}}\right).

Furthermore,

\mathbb{E}\bm{Z}_{n}=\bm{0},\qquad\mathbb{E}\|\bm{Z}_{n}\|_{2}^{2}=p-\|\bm{\gamma}\|_{2}^{2},\qquad\mathbb{E}(\bm{\gamma}^{\top}\bm{Z}_{n})^{2}\leq C\|\bm{\gamma}\|_{2}^{2},

\mathrm{Var}(\|\bm{Z}_{n}\|_{2}^{2})\leq C\left(p+\frac{p^{2}}{n}\right).

Thus

\|\bm{Z}_{n}\|_{2}^{2}=p+O_{p}\left(\sqrt{p}+\frac{p}{\sqrt{n}}\right),\qquad\bm{\gamma}^{\top}\bm{Z}_{n}=O_{p}(\|\bm{\gamma}\|_{2}).

Consequently,

T_{\rm sum}=n\|\bm{g}_{n}^{(1),\rm or}\|_{2}^{2}=\mathcal{S}_{n}+p+O_{p}\left(\sqrt{n}\|\bm{\gamma}\|_{2}+\sqrt{p}+\frac{p}{\sqrt{n}}\right),

and

\frac{\mathcal{S}_{n}}{\sqrt{p}+p/\sqrt{n}}\to\infty\quad\Longrightarrow\quad\frac{T_{\rm sum}-p}{\sqrt{2p}}\xrightarrow{p}\infty,\quad P_{\rm sum}\xrightarrow{p}0.

For the maximum statistic,

\|\bm{g}_{n}^{(1),\rm or}-\bm{\gamma}\|_{\infty}=O_{p}\left(\sqrt{\frac{\log p}{n}}\right).

Hence

T_{\max}=n\|\bm{g}_{n}^{(1),\rm or}\|_{\infty}^{2}-2\log p+\log\log p\geq n\left(\|\bm{\gamma}\|_{\infty}-O_{p}\left(\sqrt{\frac{\log p}{n}}\right)\right)^{2}-2\log p+\log\log p.

Therefore

\frac{\mathcal{M}_{n}}{\log p}\to\infty\quad\Longrightarrow\quad T_{\max}\xrightarrow{p}\infty,\quad 0\leq P_{\max}=1-F_{G}(T_{\max})\leq\pi^{-1/2}\exp(-T_{\max}/2)\xrightarrow{p}0.

By Proposition
3.2
,

\|\hat{\bm{g}}_{n}^{(1)}-\bm{g}_{n}^{(1),\rm or}\|_{2}=O_{p}(\mathfrak{R}_{2,n}^{(1)}),\qquad\|\bm{g}_{n}^{(1),\rm or}\|_{2}=O_{p}\left(\|\bm{\gamma}\|_{2}+\sqrt{\frac{p}{n}}\right).

Thus

|\hat{T}_{\rm sum}-T_{\rm sum}|=n\left|\|\hat{\bm{g}}_{n}^{(1)}\|_{2}^{2}-\|\bm{g}_{n}^{(1),\rm or}\|_{2}^{2}\right|

\leq 2n\|\bm{g}_{n}^{(1),\rm or}\|_{2}\|\hat{\bm{g}}_{n}^{(1)}-\bm{g}_{n}^{(1),\rm or}\|_{2}+n\|\hat{\bm{g}}_{n}^{(1)}-\bm{g}_{n}^{(1),\rm or}\|_{2}^{2}

=O_{p}\left(n\left[\left(\|\bm{\gamma}\|_{2}+\sqrt{\frac{p}{n}}\right)\mathfrak{R}_{2,n}^{(1)}+\{\mathfrak{R}_{2,n}^{(1)}\}^{2}\right]\right).

The condition

\frac{n\{(\|\bm{\gamma}\|_{2}+\sqrt{p/n})\mathfrak{R}_{2,n}^{(1)}+[\mathfrak{R}_{2,n}^{(1)}]^{2}\}}{\mathcal{S}_{n}}\to 0

gives

\hat{T}_{\rm sum}-p=\mathcal{S}_{n}+O_{p}\left(\sqrt{n}\|\bm{\gamma}\|_{2}+\sqrt{p}+\frac{p}{\sqrt{n}}\right)+o_{p}(\mathcal{S}_{n}),

and hence
\hat{P}_{\rm sum}\xrightarrow{p}0
.

Similarly,

\|\bm{g}_{n}^{(1),\rm or}\|_{\infty}=O_{p}\left(\|\bm{\gamma}\|_{\infty}+\sqrt{\frac{\log p}{n}}\right),

|\hat{T}_{\max}-T_{\max}|\leq 2n\|\bm{g}_{n}^{(1),\rm or}\|_{\infty}\|\hat{\bm{g}}_{n}^{(1)}-\bm{g}_{n}^{(1),\rm or}\|_{\infty}+n\|\hat{\bm{g}}_{n}^{(1)}-\bm{g}_{n}^{(1),\rm or}\|_{\infty}^{2}

=O_{p}\left(n\left[\left(\|\bm{\gamma}\|_{\infty}+\sqrt{\frac{\log p}{n}}\right)\mathfrak{R}_{\infty,n}^{(1)}+\{\mathfrak{R}_{\infty,n}^{(1)}\}^{2}\right]\right).

The condition

\frac{n\{(\|\bm{\gamma}\|_{\infty}+\sqrt{\log p/n})\mathfrak{R}_{\infty,n}^{(1)}+[\mathfrak{R}_{\infty,n}^{(1)}]^{2}\}}{\mathcal{M}_{n}}\to 0

gives

\hat{T}_{\max}\xrightarrow{p}\infty,\qquad\hat{P}_{\max}\xrightarrow{p}0.

If
P_{a}\xrightarrow{p}0
,
P_{b}\leq 1-\eta
with probability tending to one for some
\eta\in(0,1)
, then

\frac{1}{2}\tan\{\pi(1/2-P_{a})\}+\frac{1}{2}\tan\{\pi(1/2-P_{b})\}\geq\frac{1}{2}\cot(\pi P_{a})+\frac{1}{2}\tan\{\pi(\eta-1/2)\}\xrightarrow{p}\infty,

P_{\rm cau}=\frac{1}{2}-\frac{1}{\pi}\arctan(T_{\rm cau})\xrightarrow{p}0.

The same display with
P_{a},P_{b}
replaced by the plug-in p-values gives

\hat{P}_{\rm cau}\xrightarrow{p}0.

∎

A.11
Proof of Theorem
3.8

Proof.

Put

S_{A}(\bm{U}_{0i})=s_{n}^{-1/2}\sum_{k\in A_{n}}U_{0ik},\qquad Z_{0i}=\frac{L_{0i}-m_{0,n}}{\sigma_{0,n}},\qquad Z_{1i}=\frac{L_{1i}-m_{0,n}}{\sigma_{1,n}}.

Then

Z_{1i}=\frac{\sigma_{0,n}}{\sigma_{1,n}}Z_{0i}+\frac{\delta_{n}}{\sigma_{1,n}}S_{A}(\bm{U}_{0i}),

\bm{W}_{i}=\sqrt{p}Z_{1i}\bm{U}_{0i},\qquad\bm{\gamma}=\mathbb{E}\bm{W}_{i},\qquad\bm{a}_{n}=\sqrt{n}\bm{\gamma},\qquad\bm{S}_{n}=n^{-1/2}\sum_{i=1}^{n}(\bm{W}_{i}-\bm{\gamma}).

For
j=1,\ldots,p
,

\gamma_{j}=\frac{\delta_{n}\mathbf{1}(j\in A_{n})}{\sqrt{s_{n}p}\sigma_{1,n}},\qquad a_{nj}=\sqrt{n}\gamma_{j}=\mu_{n}\mathbf{1}(j\in A_{n}).

The calibration of
\mu_{n}
gives

\frac{\delta_{n}^{2}}{p\sigma_{1,n}^{2}}=\frac{s_{n}\mu_{n}^{2}}{n},\qquad\frac{\sigma_{0,n}^{2}}{\sigma_{1,n}^{2}}=1-\frac{s_{n}\mu_{n}^{2}}{n},\qquad\|\bm{\gamma}\|_{2}^{2}=\frac{s_{n}\mu_{n}^{2}}{n}.

Because

\log s_{n}=\frac{1}{2}\log p-\log\log p+\log\ell+o(1),

\mu_{n}=u_{p}(0)-u_{s_{n}}(\eta)=(\sqrt{2}-1)\sqrt{\log p}+O\left(\frac{\log\log p}{\sqrt{\log p}}\right),

\mu_{n}^{2}=(3-2\sqrt{2})\log p+O(\log\log p),\qquad\frac{s_{n}\mu_{n}^{2}}{\sqrt{2p}}\to\theta_{S}=\frac{(3-2\sqrt{2})\ell}{\sqrt{2}},

\frac{s_{n}\mu_{n}^{2}}{n}=O\left(\frac{\sqrt{p}}{n}\right)=o(1),\qquad\frac{\delta_{n}^{2}}{p\sigma_{0,n}^{2}}=\frac{s_{n}\mu_{n}^{2}}{n-s_{n}\mu_{n}^{2}}\to 0.

Let

\bm{v}_{A}=s_{n}^{-1/2}\sum_{j\in A_{n}}\bm{e}_{j},\qquad S_{A}(\bm{U})=\bm{v}_{A}^{\top}\bm{U},\qquad\bm{U}\sim\mathrm{Unif}(\mathbb{S}^{p-1}).

Then

\mathbb{E}\{S_{A}(\bm{U})^{2}\bm{U}\bm{U}^{\top}\}=\frac{\mathbf{I}_{p}+2\bm{v}_{A}\bm{v}_{A}^{\top}}{p(p+2)}.

Hence

\mathbb{E}(\bm{W}_{i}-\bm{\gamma})(\bm{W}_{i}-\bm{\gamma})^{\top}=\frac{\sigma_{0,n}^{2}}{\sigma_{1,n}^{2}}\mathbf{I}_{p}+\frac{\delta_{n}^{2}}{\sigma_{1,n}^{2}}\frac{\mathbf{I}_{p}+2\bm{v}_{A}\bm{v}_{A}^{\top}}{p+2}-\bm{\gamma}\bm{\gamma}^{\top},

\left\|\mathbb{E}(\bm{W}_{i}-\bm{\gamma})(\bm{W}_{i}-\bm{\gamma})^{\top}-\mathbf{I}_{p}\right\|_{op}\leq C\frac{s_{n}\mu_{n}^{2}}{n}\to 0,

\left|\mathrm{tr}\mathbb{E}(\bm{W}_{i}-\bm{\gamma})(\bm{W}_{i}-\bm{\gamma})^{\top}-p\right|\leq\|\bm{\gamma}\|_{2}^{2}=O\left(\frac{\sqrt{p}}{n}\right).

Define

\bm{\xi}_{i}=\sqrt{p}Z_{0i}\bm{U}_{0i},\qquad\bm{S}_{n}^{0}=n^{-1/2}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\bm{\xi}_{i},

\bm{D}_{i}=\bm{W}_{i}-\bm{\gamma}-\bm{\xi}_{i}=\left(\frac{\sigma_{0,n}}{\sigma_{1,n}}-1\right)\bm{\xi}_{i}+\frac{\delta_{n}}{\sigma_{1,n}}\left\{\sqrt{p}S_{A}(\bm{U}_{0i})\bm{U}_{0i}-\frac{\bm{v}_{A}}{\sqrt{p}}\right\},

\bm{\Delta}_{n}=n^{-1/2}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\bm{D}_{i},\qquad\bm{S}_{n}=\bm{S}_{n}^{0}+\bm{\Delta}_{n}.

Since

\mathbb{E}\left\|\sqrt{p}S_{A}(\bm{U}_{0i})\bm{U}_{0i}-\frac{\bm{v}_{A}}{\sqrt{p}}\right\|_{2}^{2}=p\mathbb{E}S_{A}(\bm{U}_{0i})^{2}-\frac{\|\bm{v}_{A}\|_{2}^{2}}{p}=1-p^{-1},

\mathbb{E}\|\bm{D}_{i}\|_{2}^{2}\leq C\left(\frac{s_{n}\mu_{n}^{2}}{n}\right)^{2}p+C\frac{\delta_{n}^{2}}{\sigma_{1,n}^{2}}\leq C\frac{s_{n}p\mu_{n}^{2}}{n},

\|\bm{\Delta}_{n}\|_{2}=O_{p}\left(\sqrt{\frac{s_{n}p\mu_{n}^{2}}{n}}\right),\qquad\|\bm{\Delta}_{n}\|_{\infty}=O_{p}\left(\sqrt{\frac{s_{n}\mu_{n}^{2}\log p}{n}}\right).

Therefore, for each fixed
y\in\mathbb{R}
,

u_{p}(y)\|\bm{\Delta}_{n}\|_{\infty}=O_{p}\left(\sqrt{\frac{s_{n}\mu_{n}^{2}(\log p)^{2}}{n}}\right)=O_{p}\left(\frac{p^{1/4}\log p}{\sqrt{n}}\right)=o_{p}(1),

\frac{\|\bm{S}_{n}^{0}\|_{2}\|\bm{\Delta}_{n}\|_{2}}{\sqrt{p}}=O_{p}\left(\sqrt{\frac{s_{n}p\mu_{n}^{2}}{n}}\right)=O_{p}\left(\frac{p^{3/4}}{\sqrt{n}}\right)=o_{p}(1),

\frac{\|\bm{a}_{n}\|_{2}\|\bm{\Delta}_{n}\|_{2}}{\sqrt{p}}=O_{p}\left(\frac{s_{n}\mu_{n}^{2}}{\sqrt{n}}\right)=O_{p}\left(\frac{\sqrt{p}}{\sqrt{n}}\right)=o_{p}(1),

\frac{\|\bm{\Delta}_{n}\|_{2}^{2}}{\sqrt{p}}=O_{p}\left(\frac{s_{n}\sqrt{p}\mu_{n}^{2}}{n}\right)=O_{p}\left(\frac{p}{n}\right)=o_{p}(1).

The empirical correlation expansion gives

\sqrt{n}\bm{g}_{n}^{(1),\rm or}=\bm{a}_{n}+\bm{S}_{n}+\bm{e}_{n},

\|\bm{e}_{n}\|_{\infty}=O_{p}\left(\frac{\log p}{\sqrt{n}}+\mu_{n}\sqrt{\frac{\log p}{n}}\right),

\|\bm{e}_{n}\|_{2}=O_{p}\left(\frac{\sqrt{p}\log p}{\sqrt{n}}+\|\bm{a}_{n}\|_{2}\sqrt{\frac{\log p}{n}}\right).

The growth conditions imply

u_{p}(y)\|\bm{e}_{n}\|_{\infty}=o_{p}(1),

\frac{\|\bm{S}_{n}^{0}\|_{2}\|\bm{e}_{n}\|_{2}+\|\bm{a}_{n}\|_{2}\|\bm{e}_{n}\|_{2}+\|\bm{e}_{n}\|_{2}^{2}}{\sqrt{p}}=o_{p}(1).

Consequently,

\frac{T_{\rm sum}-p}{\sqrt{2p}}=\frac{\|\bm{S}_{n}^{0}+\bm{a}_{n}\|_{2}^{2}-p}{\sqrt{2p}}+o_{p}(1),

T_{\max}=\|\bm{S}_{n}^{0}+\bm{a}_{n}\|_{\infty}^{2}-2\log p+\log\log p+o_{p}(1).

Let

H_{n}^{0}=\frac{2}{n}\sum_{1\leq i<k\leq n}\bm{\xi}_{i}^{\top}\bm{\xi}_{k}.

Then

\|\bm{S}_{n}^{0}\|_{2}^{2}-p=H_{n}^{0}+\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}(\|\bm{\xi}_{i}\|_{2}^{2}-p),

\frac{1}{\sqrt{p}n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}(\|\bm{\xi}_{i}\|_{2}^{2}-p)=O_{p}\left(\sqrt{\frac{p}{n}}\right)=o_{p}(1),

\frac{2\bm{a}_{n}^{\top}\bm{S}_{n}^{0}}{\sqrt{2p}}=O_{p}\left(\sqrt{\frac{\|\bm{a}_{n}\|_{2}^{2}}{p}}\right)=O_{p}(p^{-1/4})=o_{p}(1).

Thus

\frac{T_{\rm sum}-p}{\sqrt{2p}}=\frac{H_{n}^{0}}{\sqrt{2p}}+\frac{\|\bm{a}_{n}\|_{2}^{2}}{\sqrt{2p}}+o_{p}(1)=\frac{H_{n}^{0}}{\sqrt{2p}}+\theta_{S}+o_{p}(1).

For fixed
y\in\mathbb{R}
, put

B_{0j}(y)=\{|S_{n,j}^{0}|>u_{p}(y)\},\qquad B_{1j}(y)=\{|S_{n,j}^{0}+\mu_{n}|>u_{p}(y)\}.

For fixed
d_{0},d_{1}\geq 0
and fixed disjoint coordinate sets

J_{0}\subset A_{n}^{c},\qquad J_{1}\subset A_{n},\qquad|J_{0}|=d_{0},\qquad|J_{1}|=d_{1},

define

E_{J_{0},J_{1}}(y)=\bigcap_{j\in J_{0}}B_{0j}(y)\cap\bigcap_{j\in J_{1}}B_{1j}(y).

Put

q_{0n}(y)=2\bar{\Phi}(u_{p}(y)),\qquad q_{1n}(y)=\bar{\Phi}(u_{p}(y)-\mu_{n}),\qquad J=J_{0}\cup J_{1},\qquad d=d_{0}+d_{1}.

For the fixed block
J
, use the representation

\bm{U}_{0i,J}=\sqrt{B_{i}}\,\bm{V}_{i},\qquad\bm{U}_{0i,J^{c}}=\sqrt{1-B_{i}}\,\bm{W}_{i},\qquad B_{i}\sim{\rm Beta}(d/2,(p-d)/2),

where
\bm{V}_{i}\sim\mathrm{Unif}(\mathbb{S}^{d-1})
,
\bm{W}_{i}\sim\mathrm{Unif}(\mathbb{S}^{p-d-1})
, and
B_{i}
,
